#2779. 2026 NFL Week 9: Hart’s Picks

Welcome back to the ninth installment of Hart's Picks for the 2026 NFL regular season. My Week 8 slate of predictions was my best thus far. I finished with a 12-2 record with my only losing picks being the Cowboys and Seahawks. Not too shabby! We're basically at the halfway point of the season, so I thought I'd give a review of where I stand to date.Week 1: 11-5Week 2: 10-6Week 3: 9-7Week 4: 8-8Week 5: 10-5Week 6: 7-7Week 7: 9-5Week 8: 12-2

Thus far, that's 76 correct picks and 46 wrong picks. That's good enough for 63-percent accuracy. However, that is still several points behind where I have been the past two seasons. This week there are 15 games with just the Pittsburgh Steelers and Tennessee Titans on their bye week.

With Lamar Jackson likely out for the rest of the regular season due to a broken thumb and Tyler Huntley being on injured reserve, the Ravens will be starting Joe Fagnano against the Jaguars on Thursday Night Football. However, they did strike a trade to acquire Jacoby Brissett from the Arizona Cardinals to keep their playoff hopes alive. That won't matter this week, since he won't be playing. Due to an unstable quarterback situation in Baltimore, I'll be giving Jacksonville the edge here.

This will be an international affair taking place in Madrid, Spain. The Bengals are coming off an overtime win against the Titans. Meanwhile, the Falcons were shutout by the Buccaneers in a game where Tampa Bay didn't even complete a pass to win. All things considered, I'll be taking Cincinnati in this one. I think they will light up the scoreboard.

The Browns are coming off a loss, and the Saints are fresh off their bye week with an extra week of rest and preparation. The key storyline to watch in this one is who starts at quarterback for Cleveland. Deshaun Watson is in concussion protocol, which mean Shadeur Sanders could get the nod on Sunday. If so, I'm worried about Cleveland's ability to get things done on offense. I'll be taking the Saints at home in this one due to that.

Both of these teams are coming off Week 8 losses. I'm far more confident in Andy Reid and the Chiefs' ability to bounce back from a loss at home as opposed to the Jets being able to find a way to win at Arrowhead. Sorry Jets fans, but I'm picking Kansas City in this one. But hey, you have three top ten picks currently in the 2027 NFL Draft. That's something to look forward too—unless most of the top underclassmen at QB opt to go back to school.

Both these NFC teams earned wins in Week 8. The Rams have now pulled to 4-4 on the season and aren't out of it yet. Meanwhile, the Commanders are 2-4-1 and a few games out of the lead in the NFC East. At the end of the day, I think the Rams are the much better team and will find a way to win on the road. It's an absolute must to keep their playoff hopes alive in a tough NFC.

The Cowboys and Colts have certainly underwhelmed thus far. Both teams have failed to meet the expectations many fans and analysts had prior to the regular season. If Dallas wants to keep their playoff hopes and NFC East title chances alive, this is a must-win road game against the Colts who seem determined to acquire as much draft capital as possible to embark on a complete rebuild. I'll be taking another road team and am picking Dallas.

Each week, there seems to be a game I think is a surefire win that winds up backfiring on me. Looking at this week's slate of games, this is about as close to an easy pick for a matchup I can find. I think the Lions roll on offense and cause a lot of problems for a Dolphins' defense that is one of the worst in the NFL.

They haven't been playing their best football, but the Broncos continue to find ways to win. They have a four-game winning streak and all the momentum heading into their Week 9 road matchup versus the Panthers. I think Denver's offense will run the ball with authority and dictate the pace of the game to earn their seventh win of the season on Sunday.

The Eagles have been an absolute flop this season and have lost six in a row. Meanwhile, the Giants have been one of the league's surprise teams and lead the NFC East. It hasn't always been pretty, but they've been finding ways to win and are pretty balanced on offense and defense. I'm getting a bit wild here picking road teams, but I like the Giants in this one.

The Texans are a bad football team at 2-5. There is no doubt they have woefully underperformed expectations. Quite honestly, the only other team in AFC that has been more disappointing are the winless Chargers at 0-7. I expect the Texans to get back on track here and take care of business on the road since the Chargers are terrible.

The Raiders are back to being a .500 ball cup. On the flip side, the 49ers had an extra week of preparation after their Week 7 loss to the Falcons to gameplan for the Raiders. I think that is going to make all the difference here. I just don't see them dropping another game to an underdog. I'll take Kyle Shanahan's crew.

This is one of my favorite matchups of Week 9. The Packers and Patriots are both 5-3 and coming off Week 8 victories. I feel they are evenly matched. It could definitely go either way, though I'll be giving the Patriots the nod here because of their homefield advantage.

The Cardinals were able to shock the Cowboys last weekend. I honestly didn't see that coming. Meanwhile, the Seahawks lost to the Bears in primetime—the other matchup I failed to get right last week. At 4-4, the Seahawks are starting to slip in the NFC West. They need this victory to get back on the right track. I'm confident they will pull out a victory at home. But Sam Darnold needs to perform better, and they have to start running the ball effectively.

The Buccaneers may have been able to pull off a win last weekend without completing a pass. However, they are going to have to be firing on all cylinders to keep up with a top-notch Chicago offense. I'm not sure that's possible. Caleb Williams is really hitting his stride and playing well. I think Chicago won't have any issues beating Tampa Bay at home.

The Vikings were the last unbeaten team in the NFL until the Lions beat them last weekend. The Bills were able to obliterate the Ravens and are now 6-1 and the top team in the AFC. I'm excited to see how Brian Flores tries to combat Josh Allen and the Bills' offense. That'll be great to see in primetime. Though I remain confident Allen and the Bills can do enough to win on the road in Minneapolis.

#2778. Neuer makes Champions League final admission after Bayern's Atlético triumph

Bayern Munich captain Manuel Neuer was full of praise for his side after their 2-0 victory over Atlético Madrid, with goals from Lennart Karl and Konrad Laimer securing all three points in the Spanish capital.

The 40-year-old returned between the posts after missing Bayern's previous three matches through injury, while assistant coach Aaron Danks took charge in the absence of Vincent Kompany.

Speaking to Amazon Prime after the final whistle, Neuer felt Bayern had been in complete control despite their recent injury problems.

"I'd say it was total dominance from the first minute to the 93rd. Whatever Atlético threw at us, we always had an answer.

"And we're incredibly happy, especially considering the circumstances and the fact that we've had to make some adjustments due to injuries and everything that's happened at FC Bayern in recent days.

"But I think we stuck together, gave everything we had, and made sure we could enjoy a team performance like this today. It was exceptional."

The Bayern captain also acknowledged that not everyone had expected such a convincing performance against Diego Simeone's side, particularly away from home.

"We always stick together, stand united and have an answer to everything. I think there were probably some people who doubted whether we could go away to a good team and get a result today, especially given how difficult it is to win here.

"Atlético are also just two points behind Barça in the league. So not everyone necessarily expected this. To deliver a performance like that and respond to those doubts is sensational."

Atléti's Metropolitano will also host the Champions League final on 5 June 2027, potentially offering Neuer the opportunity to finish his career on Europe's biggest stage if Bayern were to make the final at the end of the season.

Asked whether the stadium would be a fitting venue to play his final match should he decide to retire at the end of the season, Neuer responded: "Yes, absolutely. And perhaps winning here now is also a good omen.

"Of course, it's a tough, challenging road for all of us, for all the teams who are usually favourites and contenders to make it to the final here.

"We believe we can do it. We have what it takes, and we'll give everything to make it happen."

#2777. Tyler Kolek gets backup point guard nod in Knicks’ blowout victory

The Knicks' pecking order at backup point guard is fluid.

It was Tyler Kolek, not Jose Alvarado, who was the first point guard off the bench to replace Jalen Brunson during the second quarter of the Knicks' 135-105 rout of the Bulls on Wednesday night at Madison Square Garden.

Kolek played six minutes in that second quarter as the Knicks increased their lead from 22 points to 29.

And Kolek — not Alvarado — returned in the third quarter as well. Alvarado did not play until garbage time in the fourth quarter.

Kolek filled in well when Alvarado missed four games due to a sprained ankle.

But coach Mike Brown, before Alvarado returned Monday against the Nets, said that he would remain the backup point guard and not be punished in the rotation for being injured.

Now, though, the pendulum has swung back to Kolek.

Tyler Kolek, who had five assists in 11 minutes off the bench, drives past Leonard Miller during the Knicks' 135-105 blowout win over the Bulls on Nov. 4, 2026 at Madison Square Garden. AP

"I said this before, every position coming off the bench is a competition, all the time," Brown said. "Nobody's minutes are set in stone. … Jose had the first opportunity in our last game. And then I gave Tyler an opportunity in the second half [against the Nets] and Tyler played well. So I'm gonna go with Tyler. And if I think I need to make a change because Tyler's not getting it done in my eyes, then I'll go back to Jose."

Kolek did not score — and did not attempt a shot — Wednesday but had five assists and no turnovers. Alvarado missed all four shots he took and had two turnovers.

"Both those guys helped us win at a high level [last year]," Brown said. "Tyler helped us win the NBA Cup against San Antonio in Vegas. And Jose helped us win the Finals. So either one of those guys, we feel, can help us in a lot of good ways at any time so they just have to both stay ready."

A 30-point shellacking paved the way for Dalton Knecht to make his Knicks debut.

With Josh Hart missing Wednesday's game due to right ankle soreness, Knecht was called up from the Westchester Knicks and recorded 10 points and seven rebounds in 16 minutes against the Bulls. A second-quarter putback dunk and third-quarter chase-down block drew loud reactions from the MSG crowd.

"He's a scorer. He's not just a shooter. A lot of people think he's a shooter because he's white," Brown joked. "I said it for you, I hope I don't get in trouble. He can go get it."

Knecht had been playing in the G-League since the Knicks signed him to a two-way deal last month.

Brown said he is "not concerned" when asked about Hart's injury outlook.

Hart missed the team's preseason finale against the Raptors due to right ankle soreness and missed time last year due to the same issue.

#2776. New York Jets News: Geno Smith Fires off ‘Believe’ Message Before Chiefs Contest

The New York Jets are 2-6. Plenty of people have written in permanent marker that the Jets will inevitably be 2-8 if we fast-forward two weeks.

Up next is a road game against the Kansas City Chiefs and a home tilt versus the Buffalo Bills.

Don't tell that noise to Jets starting quarterback Geno Smith.

"For me, again, it doesn't matter what anyone else thinks. It's about what we think and what we believe. We believe we can get it done, so again, we know it's in a tough environment; they're a historically great team, and they've done it at a high level for a long time, so we know we got our work cut out for us, but I believe in the guys in this locker room, I believe in our coaches, and I think right now we're developing and putting together a plan to go out there and get it done."

That's the right mentality to have. If the Jets are going to upset the Chiefs, who are a 10-point favorite at home, they have to believe in themselves. The green and white have the pieces in place to pull off the upset. They have offensive pieces to be able to score points, but with how the Jets' defense has performed, they might need to win this track meet 55-50.

#2774. Charles Barkley wasn’t impressed with heated 76ers-Pistons scuffle: ‘Been kissed harder’

Call Charles Barkley unimpressed with the brouhaha in one of the NBA's marquee games on Wednesday night.

The 76ers' Joel Embiid and the Pistons' Tolu Smith III got into it under the basket with 1:25 left to play in the first half in Detroit. The two big men were battling hard for position in the paint as Philly's Jaylen Brown drove to the basket.

Brown was fouled, but Embiid jumped to collect the missed shot.

Tolu Smith (35) and Joel Embiid (21) scuffle during the 76ers' 112-1016 win over the Pistons on Nov. 4, 2026 in Detroit. AP Photo/Paul Sancya

Smith then decided to knock the ball out of Embiid's hands, and it sparked a light shoving match before their respective teammates and coaches came onto the court to pull them apart and keep things from escalating.

The action did little for the "Inside the NBA" crew after Shaquille O'Neal joked he'd "Call the cops" on them. Barkley quickly shot back, feeling the "police presence" O'Neal was calling for wasn't needed in this case.

"I've been kissed harder," Barkley responded.

Kenny Smith saw an opening to rag on his colleague a bit and took it.

"Stop telling us about your love life," Smith replied.

Javonte Green (31) separates Tolu Smith (35) and Joel Embiid (21) scuffle during the 76ers' win over the Pistons. AP Photo/Paul Sancya

Barkley, Smith and O'Neal played a much more physical era of basketball than today's game, so it's understandable why that fight was underwhelming to them.

Embiid, who was given a technical foul for initiating the altercation, was involved in another odd incident earlier in the game when he faked a shot and it appeared his leg hit Cade Cunningham low as he passed the ball.

The veteran Embiid had a monster game in the 112-106 win that moved the SIxers to 8-1, scoring 26 points and grabbing 15 rebounds.

Tolu Smith (35) and Joel Embiid (21) scuffle during the 76ers' win over the Pistons. AP Photo/Paul Sancya

Brown had 30 points, and LeBron James chipped in 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists. The new-look 76ers have the most wins in the NBA.

#2772. Raiders OL will absolutely have their hands full with 49ers DL

The Las Vegas Raiders ended a four-game losing streak by defeating the New York Jets in Week 8. They'll face a stiff matchup versus the San Francisco 49ers in Week 9. The offensive line will especially have a difficult matchup with the 49ers' defensive line.

It's fair to say the Raiders' offensive line has underperformed this season. The run-game has especially been underwhelming. The Raiders are already on their third starting right guard, with rookie Trey Zuhn receiving the latest opportunity. None of those decisions have been injury related.

The 49ers have registered just 15 sacks in seven games. The advanced metrics indicate they've been better than that. The Raiders have allowed 11 sacks, with only three offenses allowing fewer sacks.

The 49ers' pass-rush-win-rate is 49%, according to ESPN Analytics. That ranks 20th in the league. Meanwhile, the Raiders' pass-blocking-win-rate is 63%, second-best among NFL offensive lines. The 49ers have talented pass rushers such as Nick Bosa, DeForest Buckner (recently acquired via trade), Mykel Williams, Keion White, and Romello Height. The matchup won't be straightforward.

Some of the 49ers' pass-rushing metrics will definitely improve now that they've traded for Buckner. Many metrics favor the Raiders' offensive line, but the challenge will be bigger than some realize.

#2770. The 'Peanut Punch' is part of NFL's forced-fumble surge. What is it?

Pittsburgh Steelers defensive back Jamel Dean had his eyes in the backfield when New Orleans Saints quarterback Tyler Shough took the shotgun snap, faked the handoff and darted for the right pylon.

The Steelers were protecting a seven-point lead in a game they eventually won 16-10. Dean matched Shough's effort to turn the corner and reach the end zone by running parallel to the goal line. Then he made the calculated decision that Shough would dive for the pylon. Once the second-year signal-caller "declared," as Dean put it, Dean punched where he guessed the ball would be in that critical moment of intersection and collision.

What happened next has played out across the NFL all season long. The ball came loose, hit the pylon (to be ruled a touchback) and Dean turned a potential game-tying play into a game-saving one because of the prevalence of the "Peanut Punch," named after former Chicago Bears cornerback Charles "Peanut" Tillman who perfected the move and harassed ballcarriers for more than a decade.

"Now it's almost like they're expecting me to get the ball out, now that I've shown that I'm capable of doing it," Dean told USA TODAY Sports.

Las Vegas Raiders linebacker Nakobe Dean, who is tied for second in the league with 3 forced fumbles, said hunting the ball is drilled into every defender.

"But I guess I can say I've put a bit of an extra emphasis on it than I ever have this year," he told USA TODAY Sports.

Through eight weeks of play, forced fumbles have increased by 32.3 percent year-over-year – 2025 was an especially down year for forced fumbles. In 2026, there have been average of 1.73 forced fumbles per game. The Raiders lead all teams with 12 forced fumbles, while the New York Jets, Steelers and Cleveland Browns all enter Week 9 with 10.

Nakobe Dean doesn't necessarily classify his successful forced fumbles as punches. They are more of a slap, he said. But the tactic speaks to the league-wide uptick, Nakobe Dean said, because defenders are not sacrificing a tackle during the strip attempt.

"You see guys who have mastered getting a punch on a ball and also making a tackle, which is harder than what you would imagine if you're making a real attempt at the ball," he said.

The "Peanut Punch" is coached league-wide, and every second defender into a scrum as the tackle is being made by a teammate is always searching for the ball. Some of Nakobe Dean's college coaches at Georgia spoke about the importance, but his pro coaches have always put a greater emphasis on trying to knock the ball loose from ballcarriers.

That trend is now flowing up from the college level, Nakobe Dean said, with rookies such as Jacob Rodriguez of the Miami Dolphins having already perfected the maneuver, according to ESPN analyst Louis Riddick. Rodriguez had seven forced fumbles during his final season at Texas Tech.

"He's a turnover machine," Riddick said during draft coverage in April.

Rodriguez's first successful NFL "Peanut Punch" came during a Week 8 loss, when the linebacker flew in and punched the ball out of the cradle of New England Patriots wide receiver Efton Chism.

"You see his attempts on the ball," Nakobe Dean noted before Rodriguez popped one out.

Running the ball and controlling the clock is in vogue again. Teams are relying on multiple tight-end formations in that effort. Risk aversion in the passing game is another factor.

The new kickoff rule and the constraints placed on defenders from an officiating standpoint makes for a "very offensive game," Baltimore Ravens defensive back Kyle Hamilton said. The defense needs every opportunity to give the ball back to the offense.

"That's something that's preached around the league on a daily basis," Hamilton told USA TODAY Sports. "It's funny seeing younger guys coming into the league during camp, especially ballcarriers, realize how many guys are going to be punching at the ball at this level. There's kind of a growing period with that, with some offensive guys. It's definitely more of an emphasis at this level, I would say, than college."

Jamel Dean wasn't punching at the rate he is now at the start of his career. But he said there is a higher premium on forced fumbles in 2026.

"Interceptions don't come that often, but at least you can make an effort play on a tackle – shoot, you got more attempts at that," said Dean, who also has three forced fumbles on the season.

And turnovers are "oxygen" for NFL defenses, as Browns defensive coordinator Mike Rutenberg said while discussing one of his unit's 10 forced fumbles – this one specifically coming via a punchout – this season.

"The ball's our oxygen," he told reporters. "I'm sure I get boring saying it, but it's in our DNA. It's what we believe in. We have opportunities in practice and periods to practice it. It's what (head coach Todd Monken) believes in. It's one of the keys to winning football games, is taking the ball away."

Like Rodriguez, other players around the league have garnered a reputation for being punchers. Los Angeles Rams linebacker Nate Landman forced two fumbles by Buffalo Bills linebacker James Cook that nearly swung the outcome of an overtime "Monday Night Football" game. Atlanta Falcons safety Jessie Bates is one of the league's best at the "Peanut Punch," evidenced by his forced fumble to save a touchdown in his team's Week 8 loss to the Tampa Bay Buccaneers. But Fred Warner of the San Francisco 49ers may have the current top billing, with he and the Browns' Quincy Williams tied for the league lead in forced fumbles (4).

The "Peanut Punch" isn't going anywhere from a rule or enforcement standpoint.

"Ball security means everything in this game," NFL vice president of operations Troy Vincent said at the fall league meeting last month – sounding every bit like the former pro cornerback he is. "The onus is on the player to protect that ball."

Defensive players run the risk of drawing a penalty with a hit to the helmet or facemask. But the bigger danger is to themselves and their own hands. During training camp, Nakobe Dean said, his hand became caught in a facemask after he tried a punch. Shoulder pads, helmets, forearms, elbows – none feel good despite being the aggressor, Dean said.

"It's the price of admission … my hands sometimes be sore," he said.

Jamel Dean came across Tillman highlights about a year ago – a game in which he forced three fumbles was in his social-media algorithm – and he said to himself, "dude, he's a ballhawk, I got to start chances like him."

Tillman finished sixth all time on the NFL forced fumbles list (44, including 10 during his 2012 All-Pro season). He was not available through the Bears, although his post-playing career has been interesting itself – he became a FBI agent but left the agency in 2025 and now works for the NBA in a gambling integrity role.

Forcing fumbles is part of the Steelers' culture, Jamel Dean said. That's one way to fit in during his first year with his new organization.

Defenders are constantly looking for opportunities when the ballcarrier finds himself in a vulnerable position. The signals, Jamel Dean said, can occur when a runner is making a cut "and they get kind of real wide when they cut with their arms." A player exposing the ball while reaching or diving is another prime window for a punch. But striking while the ballcarrier is going to the ground is key, he said.

"The more attempts you get," Dean said, "the higher the chance of it actually coming out."

#2768. 3 things from Charlotte's 114-104 defeat to Brooklyn

The Charlotte Hornets dropped their fifth game in a row Wednesday night, losing at the hands of the Brooklyn Nets, who entered the matchup with a 1-6 record. Charlotte's main issues (turnovers, scoring and shooting) came to the forefront once more, as the Hornets lost 114-104. To add injury to insult, second-year forward Kon Knueppel was forced to leave his season debut after six minutes with a leg injury.

Naz Reid led the team in scoring, finishing with 28 points and nine rebounds on 11-for-18 shooting from the field. Grayson Allen finished with 22 points off the bench, 20 of which he scored in the first half. For Brooklyn, Michael Porter Jr. and Julius Randle combined to score 48 points, with rookie Mikel Brown Jr. chipping in 18 off the bench. Prior to Charlotte's matchup Friday against the Boston Celtics, here are three things to take note of.

Although the Hornets have a couple of players struggling when it comes to turnovers (Naz Reid and Dennis Schroder) and 3-point shooting (Sion James and Schroder), the blame should be spread out pretty evenly, except for maybe Grayson Allen, who's been the team's biggest bright spot early on. Wednesday night against the Nets, eight of 11 players in Charlotte's rotation committed multiple turnovers, including Knueppel, who only played six minutes. The same can be said for the Hornets' shooting struggles, as the majority of the team has struggled from long-distance. Charlotte shot 30% from deep on 40 attempts Wednesday against Brooklyn.

While many expected the Hornets to have a slow start in large part due to injuries or take a step back altogether following the departure of LaMelo Ball, the future of the team appears much more bleak than anticipated. Currently on a five-game losing streak, Charlotte's struggles don't appear to be an overnight fix. With injuries and serviceable veterans who hold solid trade value, things could potentially get worse before they get better.

The Charlotte Hornets haven't lost their identity; they just can't succeed at what made them so successful last year, which you could argue is even worse than losing your identity. Off to a horrendous start offensively to begin the season, the Hornets are faced with the decision of abandoning their 3-point-reliant offense that made them so dynamic a year ago. Currently, the Hornets are 28th in the NBA in 3-point percentage, despite ranking No. 3 in attempts. While they haven't had a large sample size when fully healthy, it may be a while until they are, with Knueppel going down in Wednesday's game. Is Charlotte's current personnel flexible enough for a change in play style? Or will the Hornets become sellers way sooner than ever anticipated?

#2766. INTERVIEW: Bayern boss Rouven Kasper takes swipe at Dortmund's 'Fan Mode' app as Carsten Cramer responds

MÜNCHEN (Bulinews): After Dortmund introduced a ‘Fan Mode’ feature in their club app to reward supporters staying off their phones during games, Bayern Munich's Chief Sales and Marketing officer, Rouven Kasper, said the Bavarians don't have the same issues in the stands.

“I never heard about this [BVB app] because we don’t have this problem [at Bayern Munich]. We don’t need to tell anyone to take their phone away because our football is attractive," Kasper said at a media round in Munchen, attended by Bulinews.com.

“This [Fan Mode app] is a marketing tool I have never had to think about. Maybe when you sit at home [watching a game the phone can be a problem], but I think in the Allianz Arena in Munich its not a problem.

“For sure, mobiles are there [all the time] and for when we have influencers. But in the stadium, so far, there has never been an internal discussion that we need to think about a loyalty system [to reduce phone usage].

“However, I will discuss this [fan mode app] with a colleague later, we will see what the benefits are and then maybe we will think about it," the seemingly bewildered yet intrigued 44-year-old Bayern chief concluded.

The following day, when informed about Kasper's opinions on the app, Borussia Dortmund CEO Carsten Cramer took time to defend the club's decision to put the loyalty scheme into operation.

“I know this is a kind of marketing activity [the Fan Zone app], but the marketing campaigns we develop should refer to the authenticity of the club," Cramer told Bulinews and other media present, referencing BVB's famous atmosphere.

“The emotion of is our unique selling proposition. That is our most important currency. Of course, we want to make more money to invest in sport and infrastructure, but our most important currency is the love people give us.

“The atmosphere at Signal Iduna Park is what makes Dortmund special. If you talk to the young players we signed in summer, one of their reasons for commitment was to play in front of this crowd.

“They wanted to see these fans give their emotions to them. Sometimes it’s necessary to say we have some disadvantages [compared to Bayern], and others but we really have to fight for our place [in the market].

“We are still in the top 10 [of sales] in Europe. The competitors are the big sides in England, plus the two or three [clubs] in Spain, Italy and PSG. It makes us kind of proud what we have reached in the last decade," he summarized.

#2764. Wolves 117, Rockets 107: The Blue Tsunami in Houston

As soon as the game tipped off, one thing was very clear: This was a completely different level of basketball compared to Monday night's game. Unique matchups (LaMelo Ball on Jabari Smith!), shot-making, and game plan discipline meant that the first half was full of back-and-forth, high-quality hoops.

Ayo Dosunmu was such a blue tsunami in transition that even Steve Kornaki had to pause what he was doing and just say, "Wow".

Dosunmu went a perfect 6/6 at the rim on his way to twelve first-half points. On the other side of the floor, Jonathan Kuminga was fantastic on Amen Thompson, playing a brand of athletic and physical basketball that the Wolves haven't been able to do before against Thompson.

The physicality was of note, as he did get a flagrant one after Sengun's face invaded his personal space. Ultimately, it may have been worth it for the repeated over-the-top crowd reaction to the replay, which was genuinely funny.

And finally, the first half story wouldn't be complete without mentioning Jaylen Clark, the certified stuff doer. He had two steals, two offensive rebounds, and a drawn offensive foul. Luckily for the Rockets, the Wolves decided they wanted to keep the game close by shooting free throws with their opposite hands, hitting on just 5/10 as the teams went into halftime tied.

The good basketball continued into the second half, though the third quarter did leave me with a pressing existential question like: "What exactly is a moving screen?" and "Why don't defensive players just run into the screener and fall down every time?"

Outside of that Marcus Smart-inspired existential crisis, both teams essentially played each other even as they headed into the final quarter with the Rockets up two.

As you can see, the start of the fourth quarter was something!

LaMelo Ball committed three fouls in about 30 seconds and put the Rockets in the bonus. But then goes out and totally redeems himself with eight straight points, part of his twelve-point fourth-quarter effort. The back-and-forth affair continued until Jonathan Kuminga drained two huge threes, which, paired with dive-on-the-floor defense and excellent ball movement on offense, would be enough to push the Wolves over the top for the 117-107 victory in Houston.

Let's just get this one out of the way. If the Wolves had lost this game, this would have been the lament in Timberwolves land. They went 10-for-19 from the line, and missing free throws has absolutely been an epidemic for this team. It didn't cost them the game tonight, but they have way too many good shooters for these 1-2 trips to the line.

+14: Points Off Turnover Advantage for the Wolves

Hello defense! From the get-go, I loved the way the Wolves matched up with the Rockets by putting Ayo on Van Fleet, McDaniels on KD, Kuminga on Amen Thompson (more on this soon), and letting LaMelo guard Jabari Smith Jr, a matchup that would force them out of a lot of their offensive sets if they wanted to take advantage of it. This all led to eighteen turnovers for the Rockets and forced them into hitting a lot of tough shots all night.

He had the perfect balance of finding his own shot, creating offense for others, and forcing Houston to bring double teams that he broke, and helped lead the Wolves to their first clutch-time victory of the season.

The seven turnovers are a reminder it wasn't perfect, but his +15 team high with 20 points, six assists, and four – count 'em, four – stocks show a player that filled the stat sheet and played winning basketball.

The Straight to Jail Award: Jonathan Kuminga

One of the most encouraging aspects of the first nine games of the season for the Wolves has been Jonathan Kuminga's defense. It's one of those things that, if it stays at this level, could be a difference maker come April. In past seasons, Amen Thompson's elite athleticism has been a matchup issue for the Wolves, but Kuminga was all over him tonight, forcing a 3-for-9 night from the field with three turnovers and a team-worst -11.

Straight to jail for Amen Thompson tonight!

Bones Hyland is just a bucket. Scoring 19 points in just 16 minutes is a needed injection of life into an offense that is missing its high-volume scorer.

He played 25 minutes!

Another solid outing from Joan Beringer! By my count, he's now stacked four-to-give good performances in a row

Trey Lyles is the last player hasn't been mentioned, but he was also solid too! He's becoming a very valuable role player that knows what he does best and stays in that zone.

#2762. Who is Joe Milton? 5 things to know about Eagles' new starting QB

The Philadelphia Eagles are turning to Joe Milton in hopes of reviving an offense that has struggled through eight games and six consecutive losses. According to ESPN's Adam Schefter and NFL Network's Mike Garafolo, Philadelphia will start Milton against the New York Giants on Sunday, replacing Tanner McKee after three consecutive losses, with the former Stanford quarterback directing the offense.

The move comes less than three weeks after the Eagles signed Milton from the Dallas Cowboys' practice squad, giving the former Tennessee standout an unexpected opportunity to become Philadelphia's third starting quarterback of the season.

Here are five things to know about the Eagles' new starting quarterback.

Milton began his college career at Michigan in 2018 and spent three seasons with the Wolverines before transferring to Tennessee. He initially competed for playing time behind other quarterbacks before earning a start in 2020.

Milton transferred to Tennessee in 2021, where he spent three seasons developing under head coach Josh Heupel.

The change of scenery eventually helped him establish himself as an NFL prospect.

Milton became Tennessee's full-time starting quarterback in 2023, completing 229 of 354 passes for 2,813 yards, 20 touchdowns and five interceptions. He also rushed for 299 yards and seven touchdowns, demonstrating the mobility that makes him an intriguing option for Philadelphia's offense.

The 6-foot-5 quarterback finished the season with 3,112 yards of total offense, helping Tennessee finish the regular season 8-4.

His exceptional arm strength became one of his defining traits during the pre-draft process, though questions about consistency and accuracy contributed to his sixth-round selection.

The Patriots selected Milton with the 193rd overall pick in the 2024 NFL draft. He made his regular-season debut against Buffalo in the 2024 season finale, completing 22 of 29 passes for 241 yards and one touchdown while rushing for another score.

New England won 23-16, giving Milton a chance to show his physical tools against NFL competition.

Despite the encouraging performance, the Patriots eventually moved in another direction at quarterback.

New England traded Milton to Dallas during the 2025 offseason, giving him another opportunity to develop behind an established starter. Milton appeared in four games for the Cowboys during the 2025 season, completing 15 of 24 passes for 183 yards, one touchdown, and two interceptions.

Dallas eventually moved him to its practice squad, where Philadelphia signed him on Oct. 16. He arrived as the Eagles dealt with uncertainty around Jalen Hurts, whose season was derailed by blood clots.

Milton now has an opportunity to establish himself with an NFC East rival.

Milton brings a different skill set than McKee, particularly with his ability to throw deep passes and extend plays outside the pocket. Those traits could give offensive coordinator Sean Mannion more options for an offense averaging 17.1 points per game.

Philadelphia has struggled to consistently create explosive plays, sustain drives, and establish its running game.

Milton's mobility could help offset some protection issues, while his arm strength creates opportunities to stretch opposing defenses. However, he must also demonstrate improved decision-making and accuracy after receiving limited regular-season opportunities during his first two NFL seasons. The Eagles enter Sunday at 2-6, making Milton's first start with Philadelphia an important audition for a quarterback trying to establish himself as a legitimate NFL starter.

This article originally appeared on Eagles Wire: Who is Joe Milton? 5 things to know about Eagles' new starting QB

#2761. Victor Wembanyama makes NBA history with 52-point, 24-rebound, 8-block game in Spurs win

Victor Wembanyama has had a heck of a career as he plays his fourth NBA season. But nothing matches what he did on Wednesday.

The San Antonio Spurs superstar set a new career high in scoring with 52 points plus 24 rebounds and 8 blocks in a 112-111 win over the Dallas Mavericks. No player in NBA history has posted those totals since blocks became an official stat in 1973, and only one other player had posted even a 50-20-5 game (Bob McAdoo, Feb 22, 1974).

To put Wembanyama's game in additional historical context:

He was the first player in NBA history to have at least 25 points, 15 rebounds and five blocks in a half.

He was the first player who had at least 50 points and 20 rebounds in a one-point victory since Wilt Chamberlain in 1964, according to ESPN

His 15-for-15 performance at the foul line in the first half tied James Harden for the most free throws without a miss in the first half during the play-by-play era since 1997-98

His eight blocks were the most for a player who had a 50-point game since blocks became official in 1973-75

He became the fifth player ever with more than one 50-point game at age 22 or younger

The game came down to the final possessions, with Wembanyama making a free throw to give the Spurs the lead and Kyrie Irving missing a would-be game-winner right before the buzzer.

It was a tough shot for Irving, who had P.J. Washington unguarded on the perimeter but opted for a contested turnaround jump shot that missed the rim.

Wembanyama was clearly on pace for history from early in the game, eventually entering the locker room after becoming the first player to post at least 25 points, 15 rebounds and 5 blocks in a single half. It was a dominant half for both him and the Spurs, who led by 17 at halftime and as much as 19 in the third quarter.

The Mavericks, however, came back with Cooper Flagg and Naji Marshall leading the charge and at one point led by three in the fourth quarter. The Spurs were in dire straits around then, as Wembanyama briefly exited the game with an apparent ankle injury.

Wembanyama returned and helped the Spurs hold on. San Antonio outscored Dallas by 23 points during his 34 minutes, and got outscored by 22 in the 14 minutes back-up center Luke Kornet was playing.

"I can still do more," Wembanyama said, per the San Antonio Express-News. "My shots weren't falling tonight, and that forces me into a style of play that is exhausting. I can do it. I mean the proof is here I can do it, but I can be much better if my shots fell."

Not only was Wembanyama temporarily hobbled, he also revealed after the game that he's been dealing with an illness. He said he was still sick Wednesday, but no longer felt as weak as he did while dealing with a virus:

"I haven't been feeling great lately. I've been sick. Today felt like I was getting my legs back. It should be more like this more often."

Wembanyama posted 12 points on 5-of-15 shooting with 11 rebounds, 5 assists and 3 blocks in his previous game, a win over the Utah Jazz. In the game directly before that, he dropped 43 in another win over the Minnesota Timberwolves. That was the second-highest scoring game of his career, and is now the third-highest.

The win improves the Spurs' record to 6-2, tied for fourth-best in a competitive Western Conference. The Mavericks are now tied for 10th at 4-4.

#2754. NFL trade deadline rumors: Lions, Bears could compete for same players

The NFL trade deadline is coming soon (next Tuesday), and with such a tightly contested NFC North, there could be an arms race on the horizon. Currently the Minnesota Vikings lead the division at 6-1, but the 5-2 Detroit Lions and 6-2 Chicago Bears are close behind, and both teams are hunting to add more talent before Tuesday's deadline. In fact, according to ESPN insider Dan Graziano, both the Lions and Bears could be hunting for the same players.

In one of his latest pieces for ESPN, Graziano said both the Lions and Bears have made calls about acquiring a cornerback.

"The Bears made their big move for [Jeffery] Simmons, but they're still poking around on potentially available cornerbacks, as are the Lions," Graziano wrote this week. "Detroit might also be in the market for help at linebacker, particularly in the wake of the terrible injury to mainstay Jack Campbell."

It's no surprise to hear that the Lions are still searching for a cornerback. The team admitted they were in the market for Joey Porter Jr. before he was eventually traded to the Dallas Cowboys. And the Lions' cornerback situation hasn't really changed since. While D.J. Reed is certainly playing better as the team's No. 1 cornerback in recent weeks, Detroit is still seeking a No. 2 cornerback while rotating through the likes of Roger McCreary, Rock Ya-Sin, Khalil Dorsey, and Ennis Rakestraw.

The Bears, who already made a big trade deadline move by sending two first-round picks and more to the Titans for defensive tackle Jeffery Simmons, have also dealt with some issues in the secondary. No. 1 cornerback Jaylon Johnson is currently ranked 103rd out of 106 qualifying cornerbacks in PFF coverage grade (32.1), while CB2 Malik Muhammad isn't faring much better (52.5, 78th).

At this point, it isn't clear which cornerbacks are available to acquire at the trade deadline. But some of the more commonly thrown around names are Eagles cornerback Riq Woolen, Commanders cornerback Mike Sainristil, Dolphins cornerback JuJu Brent, and Chargers cornerback Donte Jackson. A month ago, we also compiled a list of defensive backs that the Lions could target at the trade deadline.

We'll see if either team can pull off a move with just days remaining before the deadline.

#2753. Illini duo post career nights in season opener

CHAMPAIGN, Ill. — The Illini defeated Detroit Mercy in an 84-50 in their first showing of the season Wednesday night at State Farm Center. The orange and blue were led by one of the nation's best frontcourts, Cearah Parchment and Berry Wallace, notching a decade-old achievement.

The Illini struggled with turnovers early, 15 of their 18 total coming in the first half. However, Illinois never wavered on the boards, out-rebounding the Titans 25-13 in the first half. Wallace (6) and Parchment (5) led the way across the first two periods, in a game that was still within striking distance for the Titans.

The Illini ultimately pulled away in the second half, outscoring Detroit Mercy 48-18. Parchment and Wallace were Illinois' top rebounders, both finishing with 11 boards. Of Wallace's 11 rebounds, five came on the offensive end, a key part in creating extra possessions for the Illini. The orange and blue ended Wednesday night's matchup with an extremely positive rebound margin, doubling Detroit Mercy, 53-23 on the glass.

A major part in the Illini's victory against Kiefer Haffey's Titans was their relentless attack on the inside. Illinois outscored Detroit Mercy in the paint 38-22 on a solid 45% shooting.

Parchment had a stellar night from the field, shooting 82% on nine makes, only missing both of her three-point attempts. The sophomore standout dazzled Illini fans with a number of crafty post moves to help her squad pull away. Parchment finished the night with 22 points.

Wallace, likewise, showed a real ability to play some bully ball. The All-Big Ten First-Team selection star shot 8—of-16 from the field and also splashed two shots from beyond the arc. Wallace ended the night right behind Parchment with 21 points.

Parchment and Wallace became the first Illini teammates to post 20-point double-doubles in a contest since 2013.

Wallace has been named to the Cheryl Miller Small Forward of the Year Watchlist, as well as the Naismith Player of the Year Watchlist, an amazing feat for the junior.

The two Illini stars will continue to feed off each other's incredible versatility and look to make a deep run in the postseason this year.

Wembanyama dominated the game in every aspect. He added 24 points and 8 blocks to his 52 points. In the end, it was his final free throw (after missing four straight previous free throws) that won San Antonio the game.

That may be more indicative of how good Wembanyama was, but the team struggled mightily with the bench unit in the game.

Dylan Harper's athleticism was on full display on his way to 21 points. He had some incredible finishes over and around the Mavericks' defenders, including a huge alley-oop dunk in the first half.

San Antonio's offense has looked its best when it's forcing switches in the pick-and-roll. When small guards get onto Wembanyama, the Spurs' guards have gotten so good at finding him for lobs or easy post-up buckets. That was on full display against the Mavs.

On the night FC Bayern and VfB Stuttgart recorded contrasting results away from home, Yann Aurel Bisseck and Matheus Cunha played big roles for their respective clubs. Meanwhile, Hansi Flick's FC Barcelona and leaders PSG recorded comfortable wins over former Europa League champions.

The Serie A outfit further dampened the mood at Old Trafford when they scored twice in the first 20 minutes as Donyell Malen became the tenth player to score four goals in this season's competition.

However, Michael Carrick's men suddenly found their rhythm again thanks to Cunha’s legendary performance that ended in a red card due to an adrenaline rash. It was also a memorable day in the young career of the Germany international Bisseck.

Liverpool, Real Madrid and Real Betis have a chance to match PSG's record tomorrow, with the latter facing a Bundesliga challenge against Borussia Dortmund. RB Leipzig will host Manchester City in the other game involving a German team.

#2749. Steelers trade targets: Watch Josh Downs, Brian Thomas Jr. before NFL deadline

The list of glaring needs for Pittsburgh Steelers general manager Omar Khan ahead of the NFL trade deadline on Nov. 10 is remarkably short.

The offensive line, which has been a franchise project for years, has come together nicely since guard Brock Hoffman and rookie tackle Max Iheanachor were installed on the right side starting in Week 3. The offense has depth at running back, quarterback and tight end.

The defensive front seven is swallowing opposing running games whole at the moment. The Cleveland Browns and New Orleans Saints have totaled just 52 rushing yards total over the past two games. And the defensive secondary has been containing explosive pass plays thanks to first-year coordinator Patrick Graham's zone coverage schemes.

Required reading: Aaron Rodgers hasn't been very efficient in Steelers' 4-game winning streak

Of course, adding star power at any of those position groups could help. In terms of tangible weaknesses, however, the receiver position stands out as the one place where this team is clearly deficient right now.

Injuries are the No. 1 reason. Key offseason acquisition Michael Pittman Jr. has appeared in just three games and made limited impact because of a foot injury. Former third-round draft pick Roman Wilson has also been on the proverbial shelf for several weeks with a finger injury.

The latter still has fewer than 400 yards in his career, now in his third season, so it's not clear he could make a significant impact consistently even if he were healthy.

Veteran DK Metcalf, meanwhile, has been mostly ineffective as the team's top receiver. He's had 40 or fewer yards in five of his eight games this season. Only one receiver in football has more than his six drops. His contested-catch percentage of 39.1% ranks 17th among 21 receivers with at least 50 targets in 2026, per Pro Football Focus data.

Second-round draft pick Germie Bernard has shown some positive signs with 284 yards and a touchdown to open his first NFL season, but he has not yet developed as a meaningful downfield threat. His average depth of target (ADOT) is just 4.9 yards.

The Steelers, therefore, have a clear mandate for change at this position, should Khan be willing to part with NFL draft capital to acquire it. Here, USA TODAY Sports looks at potential trade targets and why they do - or don't make sense.

TRADE DEADLINE: NFL trade candidates include Derrick Henry and another Jets surprise

ESPN's Dan Graziano linked the Steelers to the fourth-year veteran in a report Wednesday morning. The Colts are 2-6 and look like clear sellers, and Downs is nearing the end of his rookie deal, which should make his price manageable.

The upside in his statistical profile is clear. His ADOT is 10.3 yards, which has allowed him to threaten parts of the field that Bernard has not as the team's No. 2. With 330 total receiving yards, he's only been marginally less productive than DK Metcalf, who has 378.

There's reason to be concerned that his skill set overlaps with Wilson's. Both are smaller receivers who work primarily out of the slot and rely on separation to make plays.

The Steelers can't wait on Wilson's development, though, as they sit in first place in the AFC North. If they can acquire a more proven slot option for a reasonable price, then that makes them better, whether Wilson is healthy or not.

The 2024 first-round pick just hasn't fit in coach Liam Cohen's offense. His numbers have dropped precipitously from 1,282 yards and 10 touchdowns as a rookie to 707 yards and two scores upon Cohen's arrival in 2025, and to 10 catches for 117 yards and two touchdowns this season. Trade rumors have swirled around him as a result.

He offers more size than Downs at 6-2. And at his best as a rookie, his ADOT was a robust 11.9 yards.

First-year coach Mike McCarthy would need to be confident that a change of scenery could yield immediate results. The Steelers already have enough headaches with Metcalf. They don't need a second project if they have productive names available.

The Jets star is one of the few names Khan could acquire with the clear intent to surpass Metcalf on the depth chart. He's been a 1,000-yard receiver in each of his three healthy seasons. And he has 43 catches for 527 yards and three touchdowns in 2026.

Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers was his signal caller for two of those productive seasons in New York, so there's pre-existing chemistry to potentially be mined.

The Jets have him under contract through 2030, so there's no clear need to move him, even in the midst of an abysmal 2-6 start. The Steelers would probably have to present an offer Darren Mougey could not refuse to get something done. It's questionable whether that's worthwhile for the Steelers with Metcalf under contract and uncertainty at quarterback after Rodgers retires at the end of the year.

The former Heisman Trophy winner is another good receiver on a 2-6 team that's going nowhere this year. He's more affordable than Garrett Wilson, with only two years left on his deal at a reasonable rate. He's been productive with 310 yards and two touchdowns in just four games. He's also a true downfield threat, with a career ADOT of 13.6 yards.

The four games are the problem. He's battled a hamstring injury this year, so Khan may not want to add more potential issues to the ones he already has with Pittman and Roman Wilson.

This 24-year-old should simply cost too much for the Steelers. Unlike Thomas, he's remained productive, even as the Chargers are 0-7. He's averaging 16.8 yards per catch in 2026 and a highly efficient 2.23 yards per route run.

For those reasons, Los Angeles would likely prefer to give him a nice contract extension than give him up to the Steelers for anything less than a proverbial mint. And the Steelers aren't the franchise that traditionally overpays to get what it wants.

As former coach Mike Tomlin once said: Never say never, but never.

#2747. 3 takeaways as the Mavericks’ comeback comes up just short, 112-111, at the San Antonio Spurs

The Dallas Mavericks (4-4) nearly pulled off a ridiculous comeback Wednesday night, but fell 112-111 to the San Antonio Spurs (6-2) after a strange final possession. Victor Wembanyama completely dominated the game, finishing with 52 points, 24 rebounds, and eight blocks while controlling everything Dallas tried to do around the rim. Kyrie Irving led the Mavericks with 29 points, while Cooper Flagg recovered from a brutal first half to finish with 19 points, 10 rebounds and five assists. Naji Marshall was huge off the bench with 22 points on 9-of-12 shooting, helping Flagg lead a second-half comeback that gave Dallas a chance to win on the final possession.

The Mavericks had a rough opening quarter in San Antonio, mostly because Victor Wembanyama looked completely in control while Dallas' two best players could not get anything going. Wembanyama had 15 points, eight rebounds, three steals and two blocks in just over eight minutes, while Cooper Flagg missed all seven of his shots and Kyrie Irving started 2-of-8. Morez Johnson Jr. gave Dallas some good energy off the bench, but the Mavericks shot just 31 percent and struggled to finish anything around the rim. San Antonio led 32-26 after one.

The second quarter made the matchup look even worse. Wembanyama completely erased the paint, forcing hesitations, kick-outs, and bad attempts while blocking Flagg, P.J. Washington, and Daniel Gafford in the same stretch. By halftime, he already had 29 points, 15 rebounds, and five blocks. Flagg was just 1-of-11 with four points and no assists; Kyrie had 17, and San Antonio dominated the glass 44-31 with 15 offensive rebounds. Dallas trailed 68-51 and looked completely overmatched.

The Mavericks finally showed some life in the third. They won the non-Wembanyama minutes, Flagg started to look more aggressive, and the small-ball approach gave San Antonio more problems. Kyrie helped push the comeback, Walter Clayton Jr. hit another three, and Dallas won the quarter 29-22 to cut the deficit to 90-80. Wembanyama was still everywhere and finished with 40 points, but Dallas had at least made the game competitive again.

The fourth quarter was easily Dallas' best stretch. Naji Marshall and Flagg led the comeback, with Marshall hitting huge shots and Flagg finally getting downhill and creating offense. Dallas sped the game up, went small, and did about as good a job as possible neutralizing Wembanyama, though he still made several massive plays late. Marshall's three with 1:41 left tied the game at 111, and after Wembanyama split a pair of free throws with 24 seconds left, Dallas had one final chance to win. The possession went nowhere, ending with a difficult Kyrie turnaround fadeaway with three seconds left, and the Mavericks fell 112-111.

Cooper Flagg was awful in the first half, and aside from Victor Wembanyama completely taking over, he was probably the biggest reason Dallas got buried early. Flagg went just 1-of-11 before halftime, could not get to his spots, struggled badly against San Antonio's length, and started passing up shots he normally takes. He also didn't give Dallas enough defensively, which made the performance feel even worse. The second half was completely different. Flagg finally started attacking, got out in transition, made better reads, and became one of the main reasons Dallas even had a chance to win. He finished with 19 points, 10 rebounds, and five assists, and while the 7-of-19 shooting line still wasn't pretty, most of the damage came after halftime. He and Naji Marshall led the comeback, and Flagg looked much more like the player Dallas needs him to be down the stretch. The first half was probably his worst stretch of the season, but the response mattered.

Dallas had the ball down one with 24 seconds left and a chance to win the game, and the final possession was baffling. The Mavericks waited until roughly 10 seconds remained to really initiate anything, then basically just handed the ball to Kyrie Irving and asked him to make something happen. No screen, no dribble handoff, no action to force a switch, no attempt to get Flagg involved, just a Kyrie isolation that ended in a difficult turnaround fadeaway with three seconds left. It felt like one of those old Jason Kidd end-of-game possessions where the entire offense disappears, and the plan becomes "Kyrie, go save us." That cannot be the best option. Kyrie is obviously one of the best isolation scorers in basketball, but when you have an entire timeout to draw something up, you have to make the defense work harder than that. Dusty May has been creative offensively in plenty of situations already, which made this even stranger. Dallas had a chance to steal a game after being down big, and the final possession did not give them nearly enough of a chance.

There's really no more complicated way to say it. Wembanyama was incredible.

He finished with 52 points, 24 rebounds, and eight blocks on 16-of-28 shooting, while also getting to the free-throw line 23 times. The numbers are absurd, but his impact on the game was even bigger. Dallas could not operate normally offensively because every paint touch became a problem. Drivers hesitated, bigs had shots erased, and the Mavericks were constantly forced into kick-outs or difficult attempts because Wembanyama was waiting around the rim.

Dallas did a much better job neutralizing him in the second half by playing smaller, speeding up the game, and making him defend in space, but he still found ways to dominate. He hit a huge three in the fourth, finished possessions on the glass, and scored the free throw that ultimately gave San Antonio the winning point. This was one of those games where you walk away understanding exactly why Wembanyama changes everything. Dallas nearly stole it anyway, but for most of the night, he completely dictated what the Mavericks were allowed to do.

#2744. The Jets will have the last laugh on the Colts

As this season continues to spiral, the effects are currently being felt. All of the losing has cost the Indianapolis Colts two of their franchise pillars. More could fall soon. The losing is taking its toll on fans and players alike. Sometimes there is a silver lining, though. The good thing is that it's true in this case. The bad thing is that silver lining belongs to the New York Jets.

Two first-round picks were surrendered for Sauce Gardner. Two. The first one was felt as the Colts dropped from Super Bowl contention and into the middle of the draft. That is pain most can live with. It's this year's lingering pain that has carried over and is making this dreadful season that much worse to watch. Instead of the Colts anticipating a potential top-ten pick in the first round of the draft, the Jets are sitting pretty at fourth overall.

You can't have a rainbow without the rain, and sometimes there is a pot of gold at the end of that rainbow. That isn't the case for the Colts this year. Normally, pain is rewarded with a great opportunity to start fresh, gather new cornerstones to build your franchise out of the ashes. Yes, the Colts, with whatever horrific record they finish, will still have good positioning in subsequent rounds, but that lack of a first-round selection could haunt them.

Let's not even talk about the return. As good as Gardner can be, he has missed way too much time in his short stint with the Colts to be deemed worthy of the trade. Oh, and what did the Colts throw in at the end? Adonai Mitchell. He seems to be having a bit of a resurgence after getting into Shane Steichen's doghouse and being a throw away piece. Seeing any success he has is also a tough watch.

These are the stings that can stick with a franchise for a long time. If it all holds, not having the fourth overall pick is going to make some people sick. It has already cost some their jobs. That will only compound the issue. If the Jets select a Hall of Fame player with that selection, look out. This is the state of the Colts, and the Jets will be laughing all the way to the bank

#2742. Lakers win uneven game over Kings behind 40 points from Luka Doncic

On a night when Lakers coach JJ Redick said his team was "a little flat," Luka Doncic flattened yet another opponent.

Even Doncic started slow compared to his usual first-quarter exploits, but he picked up his pace as the game progressed, dropping 40 points on the woeful Sacramento Kings to lead the Lakers to a 116-103 win Wednesday night at Golden 1 Center.

Doncic has scored 40-plus points in three straight games and in five of his last six.

So, after shooting 11 for 23 from the field and six for 13 from three-point range while he also notched nine rebounds and seven assists, Doncic was asked if he's ever had a better offensive stretch over his eight-year NBA career.

Read more: Fun, flowing, not yet flawless: Lakers' league-best offense still has room to improve

"Maybe. I don't know," he said. "I had some good stretches in my career. But obviously this could be one of the highest ones. But there's still some work to do."

Doncic had 10 points in the first quarter, a slow start for him considering he averages 15 points per game in the first 12 minutes. He had 13 in the second, 12 in the third and five in the fourth.

He even made a 360-degree three-pointer in the third.

"He's playing great right now and he's doing a great job," Redick said. "We talked about this after the game or before the game, but he's doing a great job just playing off the ball. We're trying to set off-ball screens for him. Other guys are handling (the ball). So, I think some of that workload from bringing the ball up against pressure every single possession. Like it's just not there right now, and that's very intentional on our part."

In the middle of all of this, Doncic is making history.

He became the first player in NBA history to record six consecutive games with 35-plus points, five-plus rebounds, five-plus assists and three-plus three-pointers.

The season is just two weeks old and Doncic is dominating in ways not seen very often.

When asked if he was in midseason form, Doncic shrugged.

"I don't know, honestly," he said. "Too early to tell, you know. But as I go back, I had the whole summer to recover. A lot of stuff that I couldn't do in the last maybe eight years. So that's where the energy is coming from."

Walker Kessler had a strong game with 15 points on six-for-eight shooting, eight rebounds and three blocks to aid Doncic.

Read more: Lakers newsletter: Luka Doncic's scoring reaches new universe

Austin Reaves had 21 points to help Doncic out against a Kings team that is tied with four other teams with the worst record in the league at 1-7.

They give up 124.3 points per game, the second-most in the league and teams shoot 50.5% against them, a league-high.

So, the high-scoring Lakers just had to play to their usual effort.

They began the game second in scoring (122.5) in the league and first in field-goal percentage (52%).

But the first half was a choppy affair because of all the fouls being called.

Each team was called for 12 fouls in the first half. The Lakers were called for nine fouls in the second quarter and the Kings were called for eight.

Jake LaRavia picked up his fourth foul with eight minutes and two seconds left in the second quarter.

The Lakers had committed enough fouls to put the Kings in the penalty with 9:24 left in the second quarter.

That was the kind of first half the Lakers had to endure.

But when Doncic got going in the second quarter, the Lakers played a little better.

He scored 13 of their final 15 points in the second quarter and finished the first half with 23 points.

Still, Doncic took the blame for the Lakers' lackadaisical start.

"Yeah, that was on me. I came out little sloppy. I take responsibility for that," Doncic said. "I know that my first quarter wasn't good. So I just came back in the game trying to be more aggressive, be more focused."

#2741. INTERVIEW: Carsten Cramer relaxed about Dortmund's transfer practices despite failing to sign Köln's Said El Mala

MÜNCHEN (Bulinews): In Munich last weekend, Borussia Dortmund CEO Carsten Cramer stated the Westphalian club have not blown their cover after last summer’s prolonged transfer saga for 1.FC Köln star Said El Mala.

“From our perspective, we made a huge offer to them [Köln for El Mala] which maybe five years ago we wouldn’t have paid that much for a player,” Cramer explained at a round table event for which Bulinews.com was present.

“Numbers [in transfer fees] are changing at the moment, but we won’t pay ridiculous or stupid [prices] because we want to be a solid and serious club. No financial risks will be taken for successes.

The 20-year-old was heavily linked with BVB who, according to Sky Germany and BILD, offered fees of up to €50million but were rejected. In response to this, Cramer has no fears that the club’s historically cost-effective transfer methods have been exposed.

“We never mentioned the fee [for Said El Mala] publicly, so that [€50 million offer] is still a rumour. We just said we were interested to spend a huge amount of money,” he pointed out, casting doubt over their actual offer to the Cathedral City outfit.

“It can happen that [clubs will know Dortmund can now pay more for players] but that will not influence our way of negotiating. Everyone knows we are a powerful club, and we are a financially very healthy football club.

“We don’t need to hide our strengths. If we have an opportunity to spend money on a talented player which might sound a bit too expensive, if we are convinced, we might do it. I’m not concerned about [clubs knowing we can pay more],” he added.

Late last August, Köln would agree a contract extension with their talisman El Mala until June 2031. Could Dortmund plan another approach in the future? Cramer was quick to suppress the idea and avoid further rumours developing.

“We are happy with our squad; everyone is fully convinced with it. We decided to stop our negotiations with Köln because we had good ideas in mind and not only going down the El Mala route.

“It showed everyone that Dortmund is interested to make some special signings. What we have done with [Giannis] Konstantelias, [Kostas] Karetsas and [Joey] Veerman was really progressive from our perspective.

“We spent a huge amount of money for them, and don’t forget we have [Joane] Gadou who we hired very early from Salzburg. He may be cheap given his potential, but he cost money as well.

“No decision is done regarding how we proceed in the next transfer windows. We watch the market and there is no further comment regarding any player including El Mala, however his quality still exists,” Dortmund’s CEO concluded.

#2736. Patriots opponent preview: Packers are facing injury questions of their own

After back-to-back road games, the New England Patriots will be back at Gillette Stadium this week to take on a Green Bay Packers team that has played some successful football so far this season. At 5-3, they are currently in the NFC playoff picture and have shown themselves capable of winning both at home and on the road.

What the Packers also have shown is that they are capable of winning despite facing serious personnel questions. Much like the Patriots, they have been hit hard and will be without starters or key reserves at wide receiver, along the offensive line, along the defensive line and at linebacker this Sunday.

So, what can be expected in Week 9? Let's find out.

The Packers are a good football team, they just happen to play in what is the most competitive division in football right now. Despite a 5-3 record, they are in fourth place in the NFC North; they are, however, the current owners of the seventh playoff seed in the conference — same as their opponent this week, the Patriots.

And that is not the only similarity between the two teams as a look at the key stats shows.

Offensively, the Packers function mainly due to their passing game. They are ranked top-10 in attempts, yards, touchdowns and net yard per attempt; they barely rank outside in expected points added per game. Their run game, on the other hand, has been abysmal. They are not just 32nd in EPA per rush, but also in attempts, yards and yards per attempt — a direct result of their offensive line being in shambles: they have used 11 different linemen this year, with seven of them playing more than 100 snaps.

Defensively, Green Bay has performed very well especially in one department: taking the ball away. No other unit in football has had a better percentage of drives ending in giveaways than the Packers', which registers takeaways on 16.7% of opponents' possessions. For comparison, the Patriots are giving the ball away at a rate of 17.9% — second-worst in the league.

The Patriots and Packers have followed the same pattern so far this year. Both lost their season openers but came back with a win the following week only to lose again in Week 3. They then rattled off three straight victories before a defeat in Week 7 and subsequent win in Week 8.

As for the games themselves, the Packers didn't look overly impressive so far. They barely avoided going 0-3 by beating the moribund Jets in overtime, and registered one of their wins against a Bears team led by a pair of backup quarterbacks. That said, they still have the eighth-best strength of victory.

Through the first eight weeks of the season, no quarterback has attempted more passes or thrown for more yards than Jordan Love. The seventh-year man has gone 192-for-312 (61.5%) for 2,246 yards with 16 touchdowns and six interceptions so far. He also has scored a rushing touchdown and is ranked 12th in the NFL in EPA per play (0.150).

The Packers putting the ball in Love's hand is no surprise given their aforementioned struggles running the football behind an offensive line that has been a revolving door so far. One would assume the QB himself also has been impacted by this, but his 4.3% sack rate is still fairly low and the sixth-best in football. As a consequence, Love has been able to push the ball down the field: he is tied with Drake Maye and Geno Smith as the league leader in completions of 20-plus yards (17) and tied for third in average depth of target (9.8).

Unfortunately for Love, his receiving corps has not held up its end of the bargain at times. A league-leading 23 of his passes have been dropped, resulting in the second-worst drop rate in the NFL (10.7%).

And that is despite Green Bay having some talent at the receiving positions. Christian Watson (42/708/6) and Matthew Golden (43/619/4) are among the most productive wide receiver pairings in football, while tight end Tucker Kraft (35/350/3) has also been steadily involved. Behind those three, however, there is a significant drop-off in receiving production — the passing game runs through their connection with Love.

The running game, meanwhile, doesn't run a whole lot at all. Part of it is Josh Jacobs (32/75/1) missing six games on the commissioner's exempt list due to domestic violence charges, part is neither him nor MarShawn Lloyd (57/149/2) being particularly effective behind Green Bay's O-line.

The outlook isn't much better either: starting center Sean Rhyan (ankle) and No. 2 right guard Travis Glover (knee) were absent from practice on Wednesday, while nominal right guard Jacob Monk and right tackle Zach Bako-Bewele are currently on injured reserve. Maybe some extra time coming off a mini-bye can help the unit find some chemistry, but the production on the ground so far has been poor.

Defensively, star outside linebacker Micah Parsons remains on the physically unable to perform list even after returning to practice on Wednesday. In his place, Lukas Van Ness has been the top guy on the edge: his 28 quarterback pressures are leading the team, as are his 4.5 sacks. The Packers are about more than those two, with the other edges all being heavily involved in the rotation.

On the interior, Karl Brooks, Devonte Wyatt and rookie Chris McClellan are the main three. With rotational DTs Jonathan Ford and Javon Hargrave on IR, they are hoarding snaps at the position.

Green Bay's injury questions also have hit the linebacker position: Edgerrin Cooper is out for the year, while Zaire Franklin has missed practice on Wednesday with a shin issue. If Franklin ends up missing a third straight game, Isaiah McDuffie and Ty'ron Hopper will be the main men off the ball. Hooper is one to have an eye on: his two interceptions are tied for second on the team behind only safety Xavier McKinney's three.

Speaking of McKinney, he is part of a secondary that relies heavily on three-safety sets featuring him, Evan Williams and rookie Javon Bullard. At cornerback, Keiseaon Nixon and Brandon Cisse are the starters and unlikely to leave the field in any situations. Together, the five have registered eight takeaways so far this year.

One potential area of concern for the pickers is their kicker. Rookie Trey Smack is only 11-of-16 on field goals and 16-of-19 on extra points so far this season. His combined success rate of 77.1% is the worst in the NFL among regular kickers.

Matt LaFleur has been the Packers' head coach since 2019. Not only has he gone a combined 84-49-1 over his seven-plus seasons, he also reached the playoffs six times and earned three division titles. Not only did he provide success since arriving in Green Bay, he also provided stability that extends beyond himself: offensive coordinator Adam Stenavich, for example, has been with the club since 2022.

The same cannot be said for his defensive and special teams counterparts, Jonathan Gannon and Cameron Achord — the latter a former Patriots assistant — who were both hired just this offseason. Achord is not the only New England connection in Green Bay: offensive quality control coach T.C. McCartney was the Patriots' quarterbacks coach in 2024, while outside linebackers coach, run game coordinator and defensive assistant head coach DeMarcus Covington spent eight years in Foxborough.
