#2734. Lions Could Land Former Pro Bowl CB for Surprisingly Low Draft Price

The Detroit Lions may have an opportunity to address one of their biggest defensive weaknesses without surrendering a premium NFL Draft pick.

And with the November 10 trade deadline rapidly approaching, general manager Brad Holmes might want to pay close attention.

Former Pro Bowl cornerback Riq Woolen, currently with the Philadelphia Eagles, has repeatedly surfaced as a possible trade candidate. But the latest information surrounding his potential acquisition cost makes the possibility particularly intriguing for Detroit.

According to ESPN NFL insider Jeremy Fowler, Woolen could make sense as an acquisition for a Day 3 draft selection.

That's a potentially affordable price for a 27-year-old cornerback with rare physical traits, substantial starting experience, and a Super Bowl championship on his résumé.

And for a Lions team desperately seeking more consistency in its secondary, it raises an obvious question.

If Woolen really can be acquired for a late-round pick, what exactly is Brad Holmes waiting for?

Oct 11, 2026; Tottenham, ENG; Philadelphia Eagles cornerback Riq Woolen (2) enters the field before the game against the Jacksonville Jaguars at Tottenham Hotspur Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Nathan Ray Seebeck-Imagn Images Credit: IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect (Reuters)

During ESPN's latest examination of the NFL trade market, Fowler discussed potential cornerback additions for the Chicago Bears, who are exploring defensive reinforcements despite already acquiring defensive tackle Jeffery Simmons.

As Fowler's November 4 assessment of the cornerback market explained, Woolen could be a realistic option for a team willing to part with a Day 3 selection.

That means compensation potentially coming from Rounds 4 through 7 of the NFL Draft.

There is an important distinction, however.

Fowler was discussing potential fits for Chicago, not reporting that Philadelphia has formally offered Woolen to Detroit for a specific pick.

Still, the possibility matters because the Lions are searching the same cornerback market.

Earlier trade proposals involving Woolen suggested Detroit might need to surrender a third-round selection or potentially multiple picks.

As we previously examined when discussing Woolen's potential trade value, a fourth-round pick would already represent a much more attractive investment than a third-rounder.

Now, the possibility of acquiring him with later Day 3 compensation makes the conversation even more interesting.

Holmes could potentially strengthen his defense while preserving the premium selections needed to continue building Detroit's roster.

But whether Philadelphia would actually accept that price remains unconfirmed.

Woolen isn't simply another veteran cornerback hoping to find a role.

He's one of the most physically gifted defensive backs in football.

At 6-foot-4, with elite straight-line speed, Woolen possesses the length and athleticism to challenge receivers in ways that many defensive backs simply cannot.

During his rookie season with Seattle in 2022, he intercepted six passes and earned Pro Bowl honors.

He also helped the Seahawks win Super Bowl LX before joining Philadelphia during the 2026 offseason.

Those accomplishments don't guarantee that Woolen would solve Detroit's problems, but they demonstrate the level of talent he has already displayed.

And that's particularly intriguing when considering what the Lions need.

Detroit has received excellent play from D.J. Reed, but establishing dependable coverage opposite him remains an important concern.

Aidan Hutchinson and the defensive front can pressure quarterbacks, but that pressure becomes less effective when opposing receivers consistently find openings downfield.

Woolen would give defensive coordinator Kelvin Sheppard another legitimate outside cornerback option, particularly against bigger, faster receivers.

Unlike some defensive backs being mentioned in trade speculation, Woolen has the physical profile of a true perimeter defender.

The Lions don't necessarily need another cornerback whose best work comes in the slot.

They need someone who can line up outside, challenge receivers, and help eliminate explosive passing plays.

Of course, there's a reason Woolen might be available for less than fans expect.

His 2026 season hasn't gone particularly well.

Woolen has struggled with consistency in Philadelphia, particularly with coverage discipline and surrendering big plays.

According to an NFL trade-market analysis of the Eagles' roster, Philadelphia could potentially receive a fifth-round pick while sending back a sixth-round selection in a hypothetical Woolen trade.

That proposed exchange would represent a relatively small net draft-capital investment for the acquiring team.

However, the article's proposed compensation is an independent projection, not an actual offer from Philadelphia.

He signed a one-year, $12 million agreement with the Eagles during the offseason, meaning he can become a free agent following the 2026 season.

Philadelphia would need to consider whether keeping him for the remainder of a disappointing campaign provides more value than recovering draft compensation.

From Detroit's perspective, the financial picture is more favorable than the full contract value might suggest.

Because much of Woolen's compensation was paid through bonuses, an acquiring team would generally become responsible for his remaining applicable salary rather than the entire original contract.

That could make him a relatively manageable short-term addition.

The Lions would be sacrificing a draft pick for a player who might leave after the season.

Holmes would need to determine whether several months of improved cornerback depth justify that investment.

There's another reason this situation deserves attention.

The Lions aren't the only NFC North contender exploring the cornerback market.

Chicago has also been looking into potential defensive additions, and Fowler's comments specifically identified Woolen as an option for the Bears.

What if Detroit and Chicago both view Woolen as an affordable solution?

The Bears have already demonstrated their willingness to make an aggressive move by acquiring Simmons from Tennessee.

Detroit, meanwhile, has been searching for the right defensive upgrade without unnecessarily sacrificing valuable draft capital.

As we previously examined when reviewing five potential defensive back trade targets, Woolen is among the players who could address Detroit's secondary without requiring the financial commitment associated with an established long-term star.

However, there is no confirmation that Detroit and Chicago have submitted competing offers for Woolen.

And Holmes shouldn't increase his offer simply to prevent the Bears from acquiring him.

That would defeat the entire appeal of this potential trade.

The value is in acquiring an experienced cornerback at a reasonable price.

If a bidding war pushes the compensation into premium-pick territory, Detroit should be prepared to explore other options.

There's a significant difference between trading a third-round pick for a struggling cornerback and acquiring that same player for a fifth-rounder.

The first deal would require Holmes to make a substantial bet on Woolen returning to form.

The second would offer Detroit a lower-cost opportunity to upgrade its defense during a season in which every victory could matter.

And that's why the latest draft-compensation discussion is so interesting.

Woolen wouldn't arrive in Detroit as a guaranteed shutdown cornerback.

He would be an experienced, physically gifted defender with something to prove.

That's a reasonable gamble for a team that believes it can compete for an NFC championship.

The Lions would still need to evaluate his tackling, coverage technique, ability to handle Sheppard's assignments, and potential fit alongside Reed.

They also would have to consider whether an extension makes sense or whether the acquisition would strictly be a short-term rental.

But if Philadelphia is genuinely willing to entertain a modest Day 3 return, Holmes should absolutely investigate the possibility.

My verdict: A fifth-round pick for Woolen would be a worthwhile gamble. A third-rounder would be considerably harder to justify.

And if the price really is as affordable as recent projections suggest, Detroit could have an opportunity to improve its secondary without compromising its long-term future.

With the November 10 deadline approaching, that's precisely the kind of trade Holmes should be exploring.

So if she’ll be reporting again, she’ll need to restore her own credibility. Especially given the unique set of facts that resulted in her departure from The Athletic.

Unai Emery is on the verge of signing a new long-term contract at Aston Villa, dealing a significant blow to Manchester United. Emery is extremely close to signing a new contract with Villa, as per Telegraph. The move is designed to definitively kill off mounting interest from United. Emery has been widely discussed as a top candidate to replace Carrick in the Old Trafford dugout.

Emery held advanced talks with Aston Villa chairman Nassef Sawiris on Wednesday following the club's Champions League clash at Barcelona. The meeting also included president of football operations Roberto Olabe and director of football operations Damián Vidagany.

"The discussions focused on jointly planning the club's long-term strategic direction, with positive discussions establishing a clear commitment beyond 2029 for the joint development of the club."

Emery has completely revitalised Villa since his arrival in October 2022. During his remarkable tenure, he has lifted the Europa League trophy and steered the club into the Champions League on two separate occasions.

This success has been underpinned by a strategic overhaul of the playing squad. Despite selling six starters from their Europa League final triumph over Freiburg, Villa and Olabe efficiently rebuilt the team with ten new signings, including club-record addition Nicolas Jackson.

The club's hierarchy aims to maintain this upward trajectory. They stated: "The objective is clear: to build an ambitious, competitive Aston Villa capable of taking on the biggest challenges and achievements." Both Vidagany and Olabe are also in talks over extending their respective contracts to continue the project.

While Emery commits his future to Villa, the managerial situation at United remains highly unstable. Carrick recently ended a dismal nine-match winless run with a 3-2 victory against Roma, but his fate hangs in the balance. The two clubs meet this weekend. The Villans host the Red Devils in the Premier League on Sunday.

#2729. Bills news: What to make of Buffalo’s dominant defensive performance vs. Ravens?

The Buffalo Bills defense has been much maligned so far in the 2026 NFL season, and for good reason. Heading into a Week 8 home game vs. the Baltimore Ravens, Buffalo ranked 30th in points allowed per game (28.5) and 31st in yards per game (386.8), often struggling to stop the pass and get off the field on third down.

Then, after knocking out Lamar Jackson on the Ravens first offensive play of the game, Buffalo's defense put on a show against Baltimore's backup quarterbacks, allowing only 145 yards of total offense and just 68 passing yards while logging four sacks with 12 quarterback hits and forcing three turnovers.

Bring the energy of game day wherever you go and share every reaction with fellow members of the Bills Mafia.

Seamlessly jump between communities and conversations

Faster commenting and posting experience!

Download the SB Nation app on iOS or Android.

As great as the Bills defense fared vs. Baltimore, the level of competition does have to be considered, as Buffalo did much of its damage against second-stringer Tyler Huntley (injured after only 10 snaps), and emergency QB Joe Fagnano, an undrafted rookie making his NFL debut with little preparation.

Today's edition of Buffalo Rumblinks leads off with thoughts on whether the positives shown vs. Baltimore are sustainable for the rest of the season, or whether the results are fool's gold.

Check out the news items below and then let us know your thoughts on whether Buffalo's defense has turned a corner.

All season long, the Buffalo Bills' defense has been harping on the need to communicate better. And regardless of whether it was Lamar Jackson, Tyler Huntley, or Joe Fagnano quarterbacking the Ravens, for at least one game, Buffalo's defense excelled at communicating with each other.

Additionally, Buffalo's run defense rose to the challenge, limiting future Hall of Fame running back Derrick Henry to 60 yards from scrimmage on his 13 touches, while dynamic wide receiver Zay Flowers was held without a catch on his five targets.

Greg Rousseau and Ed Oliver were constantly in Baltimore's backfield and finished with three quarterback hits apiece, while Cole Bishop came up with a strip-sack, Rousseau, Landon Jackson and T.J. Parker each added sacks, and Davison Igbinosun came away with his first career interception after Geno Stone batted away a pass.

First-year defensive coordinator Jim Leonhard tried a few new wrinkles on Sunday, calling plays from the booth instead of on the field while guiding a defense that set season-best marks in man coverage rate (54.8%), blitz rate (61.3%), and middle-of-field closed coverages (67.7%).

"We talked about it all week, just being aggressive after coming off the bye, and we looked at things we could work on," Rousseau said. "And I feel like we did a good job of going out there and starting fast. And most importantly, finishing, because we've had good games as a defense.

Despite the positives, there are still warts on defense: the play of Buffalo's linebackers has been less than stellar; the pass rush—outside of Rousseau and Deone Walker—has been inconsistent; the secondary has battled through injuries and erratic play; and penalties continue to be an issue.

"I think our defense should look at today and understand that that's what the expectation is," Brady said. "And I'm not talking about the production. I'm not talking about the takeaways and sacks. I'm talking about the style that they played, the way that they flew around."

Only time will tell whether the Bills defense is finally starting to gel under Leonhard, or whether Buffalo just took advantage of a banged-up Ravens offense.

#2727. Stats Rundown: 3 numbers to know as the Mavericks drop 112-111 heartbreaker at the San Antonio Spurs

The Dallas Mavericks (4-4) fell to the San Antonio Spurs (6-2), 112-111, in a nationally televised matchup on the road on Wednesday. The two squads met for the second time, and it came after the Mavericks' 115-100 blunder against the Sacramento Kings on Monday. The matchup went down to the wire after the Mavericks were able to turn around a 19-point deficit.

Santi Aldama, Tarik Biberovic, Max Christie, Sergio de Larrea, and Dereck Lively were out for the Mavericks. Tobias Harris was out for the Spurs.

Kyrie Irving led the Mavericks with 29 points in the loss. PJ Washington added 21 points and 10 rebounds. Victor Wembanyama had an otherworldly 52 points, 24 rebounds, eight blocks, and two steals.

Here are three numbers to know from Wednesday night's clutch loss.

PJ Washington and Naji Marshall left their mark on the game, finishing with a combined 43 points. Washington finished with 21 points and added 10 rebounds, including four offensive boards. He has been the Mavericks' best rebounder through the start of the season and was a boost to the Mavericks on the glass despite the team losing the rebounding battle to the Spurs.

Marshall contributed 22 points, with 17 of them coming in the second half as the Mavericks closed the gap on their 19-point deficit to the Spurs. It was a bounce-back game for him after a performance to forget in Monday night's loss, after returning from an illness. The Mavericks needed role players to step up as Cooper Flagg struggled early, and the forward pairing was able to answer the call to keep the Mavericks competitive despite the loss.

Kyrie Irving finished with 29 points, 7 rebounds, and 5 assists. 17 of his points came in the first half, keeping the Mavericks within reach of the game when Cooper Flagg struggled early. The Mavericks rested Irving Monday night due to knee injury management to have him ready for the matchup with San Antonio, and his early scoring was key to the Mavericks staying within reach of the game. Though his shots didn't fall late, Kyrie Irving is critical to the Mavericks' offense, allowing them to compete with some of the league's best on any given night.

Cooper Flagg's Monday night struggles flowed into Wednesday night's performance. He started the game 1-of-11 in the first half, with a questionable shot diet that only worsened his offensive struggles early. He came into the second half looking like the Cooper Flagg we know and love, and finished the second half six-of-eight. The Mavericks need Flagg to continue to play with the aggression he showed in the second half. His ability to shake off his early performance proves that he has the ability to be a star in the league. When Flagg got going, the Mavericks were able to dig themselves out of the hole they were in and have a fighting chance.

#2726. The killer mistake Bengals must avoid at 2026 NFL trade deadline

The killer mistake Bengals must avoid at 2026 NFL trade deadline appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Cincinnati Bengals are in the midst of a brutal campaign in the AFC North. While the Baltimore Ravens are struggling to start the year, the Pittsburgh Steelers and the Cleveland Browns look like strong contenders for the division title. That's why, even with a 5-2 record, Cincinnati is still just second in the AFC behind the Browns, and the Steelers are just behind them with a 5-3 record.

It's no surprise, then, that the Bengals are among the teams rumored to be looking to make some moves at the trade deadline. They're one of the teams prominently featured in the Jonathan Taylor sweepstakes, and they're also in the hunt for other players on the defensive side of the ball. However, there's a lot of debate about whether the Bengals need to go all-in on Taylor, or if they should instead focus their efforts on adding more defenders. Which of these decisions would be a mistake for Cincinnati?

The real answer is that none of these choices are inherently a bad choice for the Bengals. Cincinnati's defense has hit a rough spot in recent weeks, but there's reason to believe that this unit will perform better in the next few weeks. Their linebackers have been developing throughout the year, and Dexter Lawrence remains a force on the defensive end. The secondary still has major question marks, but the much-improved defensive line has the ability to compensate for their woes in coverage. Bolstering their offense by trading for a bona fide running back in Taylor would be a sensible and sound decision.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

On the other hand, there's also nothing wrong with the Bengals working on their defense by trading for a few players on that end. Cincinnati's run game is terrible, yes, but they've shown over the last few seasons that they're doing just fine even without explosiveness from the backfield. Giving that offensive core more leeway by adding a linebacker or a cornerback to their unit is also a wise choice for this unit. A player like, say, Zack Baun, Budda Baker, or Tariq Woolen would help shore up some of their major issues on that end of the ball.

The real mistake that the Bengals need to avoid at the trade deadline, though, is to not do anything at all. The AFC North is always full of surprises. Yes, the Bengals are the better team on paper this year against their division rivals. However, this division is notorious for punishing teams that look over their "easy" opponents. Cincinnati needs to make sure that they're putting their best foot forward this season and actually try to acquire talent for their roster.

They're already done that once this offseason. The Bengals acquired Lawrence by trading multiple first-round picks to the New York Giants. Halfway through the season, Lawrence has already made an impact on this team. However, his gravity alone isn't enough to compensate for their weaknesses on that side of the ball.

Go all-in on their offense by trading for Jonathan Taylor? Or bolster their defense further? There's only one wrong answer for the Bengals, and that's by not doing anything significant at the trade deadline.

I especially want to thank the athletic trainers and medical personnel who responded and cared for me on the field. I know those were difficult moments, and I'm incredibly grateful for the professionalism, compassion, and care you showed me. The way you handled everything and looked after me when I needed it most is something I'll never forget. From the bottom of my heart, thank you.

I also can't say enough about the incredible care I've received from the medical team at Henry Ford Hospital and the Detroit Lions medical staff. They've been amazing to me and my family throughout this process, and I'm truly grateful for everything they've done.

To my teammates, coaches, and everyone in the Lions organization, I want you to know how much I love and appreciate you. This team is a family to me, and the way everyone has been there for me means everything. I'm proud to be a Detroit Lion and couldn't ask to be surrounded by a better group of people.

One thing I've learned about Detroit is that this city is built on toughness, resilience, and grit. That's exactly how I'm going to approach this challenge.

Please continue to keep me and my family in your prayers. We feel the love, and I can't thank everyone enough.

Campbell is on the injury report as a non-participant, as the Lions have not moved him to injured reserve yet, but none of that matters. It is just great news to hear that he has full lower-body movement and is progressing with his upper body.

#2719. Players picked on form not club location - Tandy

Head coach Steve Tandy says he does not consider which clubs his players play for when he selects his Wales side.

Tandy has named 12 players from English clubs in his squad for the Nations Championship match against Japan in Cardiff, with nine in the starting XV, including six forwards.

It is understood to be a record number of players based outside of Wales in a 23-man squad.

"I just see the boys playing the best at the minute and also you put in what fits to how we want to play," said Tandy.

"Ultimately it doesn't matter where they play, it'll be the best players.

"It's never about where they play, it's more about who are playing the best rugby."

Wales opt for centre power pair against Japan

The Prem talents hunting Wales breakthroughs

Gloucester trio Dewi Lake, Jac Morgan and Max Llewellyn, join Louis Rees-Zammit (Bristol), Tomos Williams (Saracens), Nicky Smith (Sale), Archie Griffin (Bath), Dafydd Jenkins (Exeter) and Aaron Wainwright (Leicester), along with three more Prem players on the bench in Tomas Francis (Sale), Rhys Carre (Saracens) and Louis Hennessey (Bath).

It represents a significant shift since a rule - which stopped players with fewer than 60 caps being eligible if they played outside Wales - was changed in February 2023 to set the bar at just 25 caps.

Only six England-based players were in a 33-man Wales squad at the 2023 World Cup.

Tandy believes the number of non Wales-based players is an indication other leagues want Welsh talent, and that they are performing at the highest level when they go to new clubs.

"They're also experiencing different environments and receiving different stimulus.

"They're coming back with different experiences, things they've learned and plenty of motivation to represent Wales."

Tandy accepts there are logistical challenges to preparation time, with England-based players returning to their clubs for certain periods when players at Welsh regions are available to work with him.

"We have to make the best of the situation," said Tandy.

"I'd love to have more players based in Wales, that would be fantastic but that's not the situation at the moment.

"You have to keep an open mind. We can either complain about having 20 players playing overseas, or we can recognise the benefits."

The Wales coach accepts that brings a mix of positives, and some things he may potentially lose.

"We might not have as much contact with those players or be able to bring them into alignment camps as regularly," he added.

"However, some are playing in front of full stadiums every week and experiencing different styles of competition and coaching."

Since moving from Ospreys to Gloucester in the summer, Wales pair Jac Morgan and Dewi Lake face players like England and Northampton flanker Henry Pollock in the Prem. [Getty Images]

Tandy left Wales in 2018 for coaching experiences in Australia and Scotland before returning as national head coach in 2025.

"That's part of the benefit, you go somewhere else and discover new things," he said.

"From my own personal experience of going outside Wales, I learned a great deal.

"When I moved from Wales to Australia, I also had to learn different things away from the field.

"You learn more about yourself, how to look after yourself and how to prepare properly."

Lake and Morgan joined Gloucester from Ospreys, while Wainwright has linked up with Leicester.

"I think there's definitely a change in them," said Tandy.

"There's something new, like it drags your attention, as you're on a little bit more of an edge or there's tension or excitement.

"So going to new environments generally do raise that anyway because you want to go into environments and perform.

"Seeing different things and then being around some English internationals will have been great."

Tandy highlighted the experience of Wainwright, who since his move from Dragons in the summer has helped Leicester to the top of the English Prem.

"Probably playing in Leicester with sellout crowds and the challenge of working with people like Ollie Chessum, who has been on British and Irish Lions tours, the standard goes up again.

"It gives them confidence, they realise how good some of the Welsh players are.

"That's not typically Welsh either, it's probably seeing what you could be as well."

Morgan aims to show new attacking threat for Wales

Wales players in Prem doing nation proud - Smith

Tandy says despite players making their living across the border he is able to stay in contact.

"That includes travelling to see them and having phone conversations," he explained.

"Gloucester and Bath are also relatively close to Cardiff, so we can maintain those relationships throughout the year, not just during international campaigns.

"I also speak regularly with their head coaches to understand how they're progressing, where they're performing well and which areas they need to develop."

He says it is about showing players that the national set-up cares about them, their development and environment.

"We discuss their rugby, what they're learning and whether they need additional support.

"Once they return to the national environment, we can challenge them more effectively because we understand where they are."

#2717. Bills Mafia’s Week 9 rooting interests come with a few sacrifices

That was an interesting weekend of football. The Buffalo Bills won, although I'm still trying to figure out how. Seems like that's happened a lot this season, but, hey, no one ever claimed being a member of Bills Mafia was easy.

Anyway, the Bills won. The Chiefs lost. And, as tough as being a Bills fan can be, there may have been a little sweetness in seeing Patrick Mahomes holding the ball when the clock hit zero, unable to get the job done.

Really, the only thing that could have made Week 8 any better would have been a Patriots loss. But they say beggars can't be choosers, and when it comes to New England, the only team to have bested the Bills up to this point, we have to be beggars. At least until Week 13, when Allen and Co. get a chance to even the score.

When the dust settled and the lights went out in stadiums across the league Sunday night, the Buffalo Bills were sitting alone atop the AFC. And that sure looked nice.

So, let's take a look at who we, as members of Bills Mafia, need to be rooting for this weekend. After all, there's a lot of football to be played before the Bills take the field Monday night, and we need something to do while we wait.

_____________________________________________________________________________

_____________________________________________________________________________

Buffalo Bills at Minnesota Vikings Root for: Bills

The Bills have worked their way to the top of the AFC, and now the job is to stay there. A win in Minnesota would keep Buffalo in control of its own path toward the No. 1 seed, regardless of what happens around the league on Sunday. Go Bills!

Green Bay Packers at New England Patriots Root for: Packers

The Patriots are the only team to have beaten Buffalo this season, and they're still the biggest threat to the Bills in the AFC East. New England also holds the head-to-head tiebreaker, at least until the two meet again in Week 13. A Packers win would give Buffalo a little more breathing room at the top of the division. Go Pack Go!

Detroit Lions at Miami Dolphins Root for: Lions

Miami is sitting at the bottom of the AFC East with just one win, and there's no reason to help the Dolphins climb out of that hole. A Detroit win keeps another division rival further behind Buffalo, and that's good enough for us.

New York Jets at Kansas City Chiefs Root for: Jets

Yes, we're rooting for the Jets. Kansas City is still a threat to Buffalo's hold on the top spot in the AFC, and another Chiefs loss would go a long way toward keeping the Bills there. The Jets, meanwhile, are far enough behind Buffalo that we can afford to let them have this one. Just this once. J-E-T-S! JETS! JETS! JETS!

_____________________________________________________________________________

Jacksonville Jaguars at Baltimore Ravens Root for: Ravens

Jacksonville is a little too close to Buffalo in the AFC standings for comfort, so we'd like to see the Jaguars pick up another loss. And since the Bills just beat Baltimore, a Ravens win gives Buffalo a little boost in strength of victory. Two birds, one stone. And we don't even have to like the Ravens — or whoever they have under center.

Cincinnati Bengals at Atlanta Falcons Root for: Falcons

The Bengals are sitting at 5-2, and that's a little closer to the top of the AFC than we'd like. Atlanta can do us a favor here by handing Cincinnati its third loss. Nothing complicated about this one.

Denver Broncos at Carolina Panthers Root for: Panthers

Denver is sitting right behind Buffalo in the AFC standings, and we'd really like to put another game between the two. Yes, a Broncos loss hurts the Bills' strength of schedule, but with Buffalo and Denver set for a Christmas Day showdown, we'd be happy to see the Panthers play the part of the Grinch in November.

Dallas Cowboys at Indianapolis Colts Root for: Colts

The Colts aren't going anywhere in the AFC standings right now, but that doesn't mean they can't do us a favor. Indianapolis traded its 2027 first-round pick to the Jets, and the more games the Colts win, the worse that pick gets. The last thing we need is New York getting its hands on a franchise-changing player with the first pick in the draft.

Cleveland Browns at New Orleans Saints Root for: Saints

The Browns are sitting at 5-3, and that's close enough to the top of the AFC for us to want them to stay right where they are. Or, better yet, take another step backward. New Orleans hasn't exactly been setting the world on fire, but we're not asking the Saints to win the Super Bowl. Just one game on Sunday will do.

Houston Texans at Los Angeles Chargers Root for: Chargers

The Chargers are 0-7, and the last thing we need is for them to turn that miserable season into the first overall pick in next year's draft. So, we're rooting for Los Angeles to finally get a win. Come on, Chargers. Surely you can manage just one.

Las Vegas Raiders at San Francisco 49ers Root for: Raiders

Buffalo already has a win over Las Vegas in the books, so another Raiders win would give the Bills a little help with strength of victory. Strange how quickly a team can go from an opponent to a temporary friend.

_____________________________________________________________________________

Los Angeles Rams over Washington Commanders — Strength of Victory

Chicago Bears over Tampa Bay Buccaneers — Strength of schedule.

Games that don't move the needle for Buffalo:

_____________________________________________________________________________

Being at the top of the AFC is a pretty good place to be. The problem is, now we're going to spend the rest of the season looking over our shoulders to see who's coming for that No. 1 seed.

This weekend, we'll be keeping one eye on the Patriots, another on the Chiefs, and somehow finding a way to root for the Jets without feeling too dirty about it. Being a Bills fan sometimes requires sacrifices.

And if Sunday goes our way, a Buffalo win on Monday night would just be the icing on the cake.

#2715. Lennart Karl sparks controversy with provocative "blah-blah" celebration in Bayern's win over Atletico

Following Bayern Munich's 2-0 victory over Atletico Madrid, Lennart Karl was once again one of the main talking points at the German club.

Not only did the 18-year-old score the opening goal in Bayern's victory, but he also celebrated in controversial fashion, leaving plenty of room for interpretation as he raised both hands towards his ears and mimicked talking with his fingers.

It seems fair to assume that Karl's so-called “blah-blah” gesture was aimed at his critics. But was he also taking aim at Bayern supervisory board chairman Uli Hoeneß, who recently called on the youngster to show more humility, and board member for sport Max Eberl, who had pointed to a recent “dip in form and one or two missteps”?

Speaking to the media after the game, Eberl claimed he had “honestly not seen Karl's celebration at all”.

"I was celebrating too, and when that happens, I don't look at what the others are doing. I'm happy for him that we scored the goal," the Bayern chief explained, according to t-online.

He added that Karl is “an important player” for Bayern and that the club's hierarchy “have never questioned that”.

Midfielder Tom Bischof smiled and admitted he did not know what the celebration meant, while captain Manuel Neuer also avoided getting drawn into the controversy.

"There's nothing to discuss and nothing to make an issue out of," Neuer insisted.

Bayern assistant coach Aaron Danks, who was once again in charge in Vincent Kompany's absence, also shared his thoughts on Karl's situation and celebration.

“There's been a lot of noise around Lenny recently," Danks said, as quoted by iMiaSanMia.

"As a young player, I think he showed great character and great mental strength. He has a good belief in himself. And his performance today was great, capped off with a really good goal. I thought he finished it excellent, lifting it over the goalkeeper.

“I haven't seen the celebration. We're really pleased for Lenny, pleased for the group, and pleased with the team's performance tonight.”

#2714. Maxx Crosby: I love being a Raider, and this is where I want to be

The Raiders have traded Maxx Crosby once, but the Ravens reneged on the deal.

A report this week indicated that the Raiders have “no plans” to trade the edge rusher again, and he doesn’t want them to.

“I love being a Raider. I’ve said that a million times,” Crosby said Wednesday, via video from Raider Nation Radio. “This is where I want to be. Super excited, obviously for the present, but also for what’s to come.”

The Ravens sent two first-round picks to the Raiders for Crosby. The Ravens, though, failed him on his physical and sent him back to Las Vegas, because of long-term concerns about Crosby’s surgically repaired left knee.

Crosby, 29, likely could still bring two first-round picks to the Raiders. He has 22 tackles, three sacks, a forced fumble, a pass defensed and 18 quarterback hits this season.

Crosby has played 88 percent of the team’s defensive snaps.

He expressed optimism at where the Raiders are headed short term and long term.

“I’m not thinking too far down the road. I’m being present,” Crosby said. “We’re 4-4. We’ve got a really good football team. I love this locker room. I love the guys. We have great leadership. Klint [Kubiak] is a really good coach. We have Robby [Leonard], who is a first time being a DC. He’s doing a hell of a job. We’ve got a lot of new faces, new pieces, a lot of young guys as well.

“I love where we’re going. We’ve got a lot of work to do. We know that. That’s the beauty in it. Everyone can say whatever they want. We had this conversation last week [after a 35-21 loss to the Rams], and it was like the world is ending, and now it’s all these positive vibes. #2712. Eagles-Commanders somehow breaks 19 million viewers

When George Costanza pitched the show about nothing to NBC president Russell Dalrymple, Dalrymple asked, “Well, why am I watching it?”

More than 30 years later, that sentiment still applies. As evidenced by the fact that, per Nielsen, 19.015 million (on average) watched Sunday night’s snoozefest between the Eagles and Commanders on the network that Dalrymple once ran.

It’s an amazing number, given that it was the 2-5 Eagles against the 1-4-1 Commanders. The game itself wasn’t particularly compelling, and it followed afternoon windows that culminated in a down-to-the-wire showdown between the Chiefs and the Broncos.

The league could have flexed the game out of the evening window. It chose not to. In the end, the not-ready-for-prime-time game managed to attract far more viewers than anyone would have expected.

It helped that there wasn’t a 9:30 a.m. ET game in Week 8. Fourteen straight hours of football is a lot, even for the most ardent fan.

Then again, Commanders-Eagles was a lot. Even for the most ardent fan. And more than 19 million nevertheless watched the game.

#2711. Packers-Patriots Preview: Christian Gonzalez defines New England’s defense

Last week, the Carolina Panthers sat in split-safety coverages against the Green Bay Packers, forcing the team to run the football or take checkdown passes routinely — just like their matchup in 2025. While this didn't stop the Packers' offense, which still had a 100-yard receiver in Christian Watson despite his in-game trip to the hospital for a rib injury, it did mean that the offense was a lot less explosive than it was in prior weeks.

Don't expect that same strategy from the New England Patriots this week, as it's not really their identity to sit back and just let offense happen to them, hoping that the opposing team will trip up at some point.

According to Next Gen Stats, the Patriots have sent max pressure (six or more pass rushers) with man coverage behind it 37 times already this season, the most in the league. On those looks, they've posted five sacks and allowed just 14 completions on 31 pass attempts (45 percent) for 123 yards (4.0 yards per attempt), with one interception and no touchdowns surrendered. The overall conversion rate on those players is 21.6 percent, which is the best mark in the league in these situations.

In contrast, New England has only sent 13 of these "max pressure" looks out of zone in 2026, surrendering 9 of 13 in the air for 131 yards (10 yards per attempt) and a touchdown. In short, the Patriots' man coverage looks have been better for them than zone coverage looks this year, especially when they're trying to speed quarterbacks up.

Patriots cornerback Christian Gonzalez, their All-Pro defender, helps the team unlock that man coverage identity when so many other NFL teams are leaning out of the man coverage game. When Gonzalez has been on the field, the team has played man coverage on 39 percent of their defensive snaps, compared to just 29 percent when he's not in the game.

Packers quarterback Jordan Love has done his most damage this year against the blitz when he's had either Christian Watson or Matthew Golden in one-on-one looks, but unlike a lot of the teams Green Bay has played this year, the Patriots are just as comfortable playing man as they are playing zone, even behind a blitz.

Gonzalez travels, as he's followed around specific receivers in man coverage at least 10 times in three separate games, a number that leads the NFL. For perspective, neither Watson nor Golden has been seen man coverage 10 times by a single defender in a single game this entire season.

Something has to break here. This is going to be the most important matchup of Sunday's game, by far. The only question is whether Gonzalez will travel with a receiver and which one of the Packers' deep threats it will be.

#2708. Florida football to start Tramell Jones Jr at QB for Oklahoma | Report

Florida football is set to make a change at starting quarterback.

The Florida Gators (5-3, 3-3 SEC) will go with Tramell Jones Jr. over Aaron Philo to start on Saturday, November 7 against Oklahoma, according to a report from Matt Zenitz of CBS Sports.

The 6-foot-0, 196-pound Jones came in relief of Philo during UF's 27-8 loss to Georgia on October 31 and led the Gators on their lone TD drive, scoring on a two-yard TD run.

Jones also led Florida to an upset win over Texas on October 17, passing for 155 yards and 2 TD while Philo sat out the game with an injured shoulder.

A Jacksonville native and former Mandarin High standout, Jones has completed 63 of 89 passes for 579 yards with 4 TDs to 3 interceptions. He's also rushed for 83 yards and a 1 TD.

Florida coach Jon Sumrall said the Gators have to play better around the quarterback in order to get is struggling offense back on track. Florida has scored less than 20 points in three of its last four games, averaging 17.8 ppg during that span.

"It would be really nice if we make the quarterback's job easier," Sumrall said. "And if we'll protect for them, we're gonna hit some more explosive plays in the passing game. The problem is, in protection, we haven't been consistent, and that's been frustrating for me."

#2706. Jim Harbaugh, Justin Herbert respond to Quentin Lake's "seeing ghosts" critique

As the football-following world tries to figure out the problem(s) with Chargers quarterback Justin Herbert, Rams safety Quentin Lake provided a scathing critique of Herbert’s most recent performance, in a 24-9 loss to the Rams.

“He was throwing deli meat out there,” Lake said. “He’s seeing ghosts. I don’t know what’s going on, but we knew we were gonna get a couple. At least one.”

On Wednesday, both Chargers coach Jim Harbaugh and Herbert himself were asked about Lake’s remarks.

“As far as anybody’s opinion I don’t see that,” Harbaugh told reporters. “Everybody’s entitled to their opinion. Not what I see.”

So what did Harbaugh see in the latest loss?

“I thought he played — did his job exceptionally well,” Harbaugh said. “You know, two throws he would have liked to have back.”

Herbert said he wasn’t aware of Lake’s comments.

“I haven’t looked into it, haven’t heard anything about it, so I can’t really make a comment about it,” Herbert told reporters. “We’re really just focused on what’s going on in the building, playing football and doing what we can and doing our best to, you know, go put our best effort out on Sunday. And that’s all that really matters. Anything outside of that is out of our control.”

Still, it’s jarring to hear an NFL defender make comments about a supposed franchise quarterback that make him seem like a Jets first-round draft pick who has yet to have the game slow down. Herbert is in the seventh year of his career. He’s making $52.5 million per year.

Something has happened this year. It’s still not clear what it is, or whether it will change. Before it can change, the Chargers need to realize there’s a problem.

And that’s the most confounding part of all of it. They’ve lost seven and won none; at times, they sound like they’ve won seven and lost none.

#2705. How the Denver Broncos can beat the Carolina Panthers

The Denver Broncos sit atop the AFC West with a 6-2 record. On Sunday, the Broncos will battle the Carolina Panthers who are 3-4 and in first place of the NFC South for their Week 9 matchup. Denver has momentum entering the game being winners of four in a row, whilst Carolina has dropped back-to-back games. Here are three ways I think the Broncos can earn their seventh victory of the season.

Last season, Denver had an effective running game in the first half of the season. Unfortunately, it tapered off greatly after J.K. Dobbins was injured. This year, Denver has experienced the opposite. It's been a relatively slow start for them establishing the run, but injuries have played a part in that this year too.

While Denver's current stable of backs isn't completely 100-percent health-wise, I think there is a huge opportunity here to revitalize their run game on Sunday. Why? Because the Panthers have one of the worst run defenses in the National Football League.

It's clearly a giant weakness for the Carolina. They are allowing nearly 5 yards per carry. Moreover, they've been dreadful in four of seven games with respect to rushing yards. They gave up 291 on the ground to the Chicago Bears, 258 to the Tampa Bay Buccaneers, 157 to the Atlanta Falcons, and 132 to the Cleveland Browns.

I'd love to see a healthy dose of Jonah Coleman in this game. The rookie running back has looked good in his limited opportunities and deserves a majority of the carriers moving forward. It's a road game, so dictating the pace of it will be essential, and enforcing a consistent rushing attack is key to that.

Whether or not the Broncos will be buyers at the trade deadline remains to be seen. However, the more opportunities Coleman gets, the easier the evaluation process for him as a player gets. And in turn, would help answer whether or not the franchise needs to trade off quality draft capital to add to the room prior to November 10th.

Believe it or not, the Panthers enter Sunday's matchup as a Top 10 offense in the National Football League. Quarterback Bryce Young has played a big part in that. He has thrown for 1,921 yards, 13 touchdowns, and has just 5 interceptions thus far in his fourth season leading their offense.

Young sported a few hot games to start the season. However, he has struggled recently. The former top-pick has 3 interceptions and just 2 touchdowns in their back-to-back losses. His accuracy has also experienced a big drop-off—completing less than 57-percent of passes the past three games.

In 2025, Young was one of the least sacked quarterbacks in the NFL with just 27. In seven games, he has already been sacked 23 times. The crazy part? Eleven of those have come in the past two games alone. Knowing that, this seems like a prime week for Denver's defensive trenches to rev up the pass rush. If they can rattle and get after Young early and often, they'll have a great shot at winning.

The Broncos are a 6-2 football team despite struggling in many key areas. One major issue for them since Week 1 has been their inability to convert on third down. The Broncos are now dead last in the NFL with under a 30-percent conversion rate.

It's true Denver has faced some really good defenses. However, that isn't an excuse for their poor play in this regard. This is arguably the biggest area the Broncos have to improve upon as they gear up for Sunday's matchup—as well as the second half stretch of the season after their bye week.

The Panthers have a bottom 5 third down defense in the NFL. Opposing teams are converting 46-percent of third downs against them each week. Head Coach Sean Payton, quarterback Bo Nix, and the rest of the offense need to find a way to sustain drives and move the field on third down.

Last season, Courtland Sutton was the go-to guy if Denver needed to move the chains. Perhaps this weekend the Broncos make the savvy veteran their main target in the passing attack when they need a big play. He has been clutch through his career in that aspect. If not, who on offense can step up and be that player?

#2703. Watch Victor Wembanyama's historic night: 52 points, 24 rebounds, eight blocks

It was a legendary performance, even by Victor Wembanyama's unreasonably lofty standards: A career-high 52 points and 24 rebounds, plus eight blocked shots.

It's been 52 years since a player put up a stat line like that — and Wembanyama did that despite fighting off a virus and tweaking his ankle in the fourth quarter.The Spurs needed every bit of an epic Wemby game to beat Dallas, 112-111.

Wembanyama went 16-of-28 from the floor and did much of his damage at the free-throw line, where he was 19-of-23. The only knock on his night was that he shot 1-of-8 from 3, but he realized that and got downhill. Wemby finished 14-of-18 in the paint.

"I can still do more," Wembanyama said postgame, via the Associated Press. "I mean, my shots weren't falling tonight and that forces me into a style of play that is exhausting. I can do it. I mean the proof is here. I can do it, but I can be much better if my shots fall. I know it's just a matter of time."

Wemby's hot night started early, he had 29 points, 15 rebounds and five blocks in the first half — the first player since the NBA's play-by-play era started in 1996 with at least 25 points, 10 rebounds and five blocks in a half.

After dropping their first two games of the season, the Spurs have rattled off five straight wins and Wembanyama has inserted himself in the too-early MVP conversation next to Luka Doncic.

#2702. INTERVIEW: Patrick Owomoyela "wasn't aiming for BVB" move until a close friend convinced him

MÜNCHEN (Bulinews) Speaking at a media event attended by Bulinews.com, Patrick Owomoyela revealed a close friend was the real reason behind his 2008 move from Werder Bremen to Borussia Dortmund.

“When I was at Werder Bremen, they were like the challenger to Bayern Munich. I was there for three years, we always came third or second, but we were right there," Owomoyela explained.

“In my time, when I signed the contract [for Dortmund] that was it, I didn’t look anywhere else. I know this is very different today, their agents have plans. Now when players sign a contract they already know where they want to go next.

"I was already enjoying my time at the club I was with [Werder Bremen]. Borussia Dortmund wasn’t looking all too fancy and sexy back then. They almost came out of bankruptcy and had lots of different coaches. I wasn’t aiming for BVB.

“When that [Dortmund’s offer], came up, I had a friend in the stadium who was playing at Dortmund. So I knew about the stadium, the energy and how cool that could be [to play in front of]. He told me about the culture and fans.

“It was Otto Addo, I grew up with him in Hamburg. Like me he was a guy from Hamburg that had an African background that made it pro. I would visit him sometimes [in Dortmund].

“He had worked with Jurgen Klopp before so he got me thinking they could be building something big. I wasn’t [planning on] going [to Dortmund] to be Bundesliga champion, that was way out of the picture at the time,” Owomoyela chuckled.

Little did he know, less than four seasons later he and Klopp would be celebrating winning back-to-back German topflight titles, as well as a 5-2 DFB-Pokal triumph over FC Bayern Munich.

The situation would be very different if Jacobs hadn’t pleaded no contest to a misdemeanor battery charge arising from an incident in May 2026. Admitting to an incident of violence against a woman makes it harder for him to credibly claim that three other women who contacted authorities about Jacobs doing the same thing are making it all up.

Still, Jacobs believes the newly-surfaced allegations won’t result in more missed time, following the six-game stint on paid leave arising from the most recent incident.

“With all the evidence that we have and that they have at this point, it’s not one of those things where I’m sitting back on my hands like, ‘OK, am I going to play this week?’” Jacobs said. “It’s like, I kind of already know what it is, so it’s like . . . it’s really just up to them to make their final decision. But like I’m not worried about feeling like I’m going to miss any more time than I already have.”

The league continues to investigate Jacobs, and it’s investigating the recent allegations, too. Through it all, the Packers have been linked on multiple occasions to a potential trade for Colts running back Jonathan Taylor. If the Packers land Taylor, it could mean that they’ll move on from Jacobs.

"It was contingent upon [Jennings] hopping on a FaceTime with Kyle Shanahan and John Lynch and saying, 'OK, yeah, I want to come back,' but he didn't. He said, 'Actually, no I want to stay here in Minnesota.'"

Patrick Mahomes says Chiefs aren't afraid to call the quarterback sneak

"There have been quarterback sneaks in games that we have put in," Mahomes shared. "We will alert and change at the line of scrimmage if they get in those Tite fronts. Obviously, that wasn't the play in that situation of the game, but it's not like we're scared to call the quarterback sneak. It's just something that we haven't done; we've been able to execute with other things."

"So I don't think coach Reid has put that on me," Mahomes clarified. "But at the end of the day, if we can't execute with the other things, I'm sure we're going to have to make changes, and that's something coach Reid is not afraid to do is make changes whenever needed."

#2698. Cardinals HC Mike LaFleur offers cautious take on Jacoby Brissett trade

Cardinals HC Mike LaFleur offers cautious take on Jacoby Brissett trade appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Jacoby Brissett's extensive NFL experience made him an attractive target for the Baltimore Ravens, as they have been dealing with multiple injuries in their quarterback room. However, Brissett's departure from the Arizona Cardinals follows a disappointing stretch with the franchise, and Arizona head coach Mike LaFleur has remained cautious when addressing the veteran quarterback's potential exit.

During a press conference, LaFleur avoided any detailed comments while saying that the situation was still being handled through the appropriate process.

"Start with obviously the news out of respect for the process and just working to get stuff finalized," LaFleur said. "I mean, I really don't have much of a comment on it, but we're going to let the process play out with that."

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Brissett entered the season with an opportunity to establish himself as Arizona's starting quarterback. However, his inconsistent performances ultimately cost him the position. The veteran recorded 10 touchdown passes and seven interceptions for the Cardinals so far this season.

The Cardinals also have a financial incentive to move on from Brissett. Arizona reworked his contract during the offseason, and trading him would allow the franchise to move forward with its new quarterback hierarchy while creating an opportunity to reshape its roster.

Meanwhile, the move comes at a difficult time for the Ravens, who suddenly find themselves dealing with a depleted quarterback room.

Lamar Jackson suffered a thumb injury that is expected to require surgery and could sideline him for the remainder of the season. Meanwhile, Tyler Huntley is also set to miss the rest of the season after sustaining a turf toe injury.

With both experienced quarterbacks unavailable, Baltimore has been forced to turn to undrafted rookie Joe Fagnano. However, acquiring Brissett would give the franchise another experienced option on their roster.

However, reportedly, the Ravens are targeting Brissett to potentially take over as their starting quarterback when they face the Los Angeles Chargers in Week 10.

#2697. Spurs hold off Mavs behind Wembanyama’s historic night

The San Antonio Spurs concluded their homestand in thrilling fashion on Wednesday night, holding off a furious second half rally by the Dallas Mavericks to earn the 112-111 victory at the Frost Bank Center behind a historic and career night from Victor Wembanyama.

The victory is the sixth straight for San Antonio, who defeated Dallas for the second time this season.

Wembanyama was fired up from pre-game introductions, pumping his team up as he went through the high-five line. From the opening tip, he was aggressive inside the paint and getting to the free throw line. He scored eight of the Spurs' first 10 points and would finish the first quarter with 15 points.

The second quarter saw both teams go on a pair of runs. With the Spurs leading 48-39, Mavericks coach Dusty May called timeout and following that, Dallas went on a 7-0 run to cut San Antonio's nine point lead down to two points. Meanwhile, later in the period, the Spurs would close the second quarter on 19-3 run to take the 68-51 advantage into the second half.

Wembanyama finished the first half with 29 points, 15 rebounds, and five blocks. The one area where Wemby struggled? His three-point shooting, just going 1-for-8 on the night.

"I can still do more," Wembanyama said after the game. "I mean, my shots weren't falling tonight and that forces me into a style of play that is exhausting. I can do it. The proof is here. I can do it, but I can be much better if my shots fell."

Both teams had a slow start to the second half, but it was Dallas that looked like a team that got a grilling at the break. The Mavericks were much more aggressive to begin the third quarter, led by Kyrie Irving and Naji Marshall. At one point, Julian Champagnie left Marshall open for a corner three. That left Spurs coach Mitch Johnson furious as he called timeout and let his team know just how frustrated he was with their effort.

The Spurs responded out of the timeout by building the lead back to 13 points, but Dallas would win the third quarter 27-22 and that set up a thrilling finish in the final 12 minutes. The Mavericks opened the fourth quarter by outscoring San Antonio 12-4, all but erasing their 19-point deficit. The final 8:45 saw both teams go back and forth exchanging the lead, but this Wembanyama would not allow the Spurs to lose on this night.

San Antonio got their star the ball at every chance down the court over the final three minutes. He had two putbacks in the final 2:39, but went just 1-for-4 from the free throw line, causing anxiety for many inside the arena. With the Spurs clinging to the 112-111 lead, Dallas had one final chance. Irving missed a contested 15-foot, turnaround jumper with three seconds remaining thanks to some great defense from De'Aaron Fox and the Spurs held on.

Wemby finished with career highs of 52 points and 24 rebounds along with eight blocks.

"I think he's settled into the season like we all have," Coach Johnson said of his star player after the game. "We're almost 10 games in. It takes a minute to get in that flow, we had to just got to a good spot in terms of team [needing] to get where their feet were. Team has potential. We're starting to play the brand of basketball we should."

Dallas was led by Irving's 29 points, seven rebounds, and five assists. Marshall added 22, while PJ Washington chipped in 21 and Cooper Flagg added 19 points for the Mavericks, who fell to 4-4 on the season.

Dylan Harper, who finished with 21 points, said postgame that he looked up to Irving growing up and to play against him is incredible, but beating him meant more.

"It was good to get the win over him today for sure," he said after the game.

In addition to Wemby's career highs, the victory for the Spurs was also the 100th in Coach Mitch Johnson's career. According to Spurs socials, Wemby presented Johnson with the game ball in the locker room after the game.

Devin Vassell and Champagnie went a combined 0-for-14 on the night. Coach Johnson said after the game he doesn't believe we'll see a game where that happens again this season.

San Antonio's bench struggled on the night, scoring just 10 points compared to 38 points from Dallas' second unit.

The Spurs were ice cold (no pun intended on throwback night) from beyond the arc, shooting just 13 percent and going 5-for-36. That will and should change as the season goes on.

#2696. Nick Bosa returns to practice with limited work

49ers defensive end Nick Bosa returned to practice Wednesday after missing the team’s past five games with a calf injury. Bosa was a limited participant.

“It’s a long year, and I kind of just reminded myself that I need to be ready for the long haul,” Bosa said, via Harrison Rich of the San Francisco Chronicle.

Bosa tore his right ACL in a Sept. 21, 2025, game against the Cardinals and underwent surgery five days later. As he was coming back this summer, Bosa experienced soreness and tendinitis in his right knee, and the 49ers backed him down until Sept. 2 when he returned to practice.

He injured his left calf while warming up in practice for Week 3.

“I must have had some type of compensation going on, especially in that first week back,” Bosa said. “I was kind of trying to push it back with the [right knee] tendinitis, so I may have been favoring [the left calf] a little bit. I was just doing my normal warm-up drills one day, and I tweaked it. Calves are a pretty long process. I’ve never had one, so it took a little longer than I expected, but I don’t even think about it anymore.”

Wide receiver Mike Evans (ribs/Achilles) remained out of practice, and running back Christian McCaffrey (hamstring) also didn’t practice.

Wide receiver Deebo Samuel (hamstring), safety Siran Neal (knee/foot) and offensive tackle Trent Williams (rest) were the other players who sat out Wednesday’s session.

Linebacker Luke Gifford (groin), cornerback Nate Hobbs (adductor), wide receiver Christian Kirk (knee), wide receiver Demarcus Robinson (ankle), safety Marques Sigle (ankle), cornerback Upton Stout (shoulder) and linebacker Garret Wallow (hamstring) were limited.

#2695. How the Chicago Bears Are Controlling the Clock This Season

One of the more notable aspects of the Chicago Bears' performance this season has been their ability to control the clock. The Bears are averaging 35:26 in time of possession, putting together extended drives, consistently moving the chains, and giving opposing offenses fewer opportunities with the football.

On Monday night, the Bears put together a 17-play, 80-yard opening drive against the Seattle Seahawks that took 8:32 off the clock.

Caleb Williams noted that sustaining drives was something the offense spent time working on during training camp and OTAs.

"We do these long-drive drills in training camp and OTAs that you hate, that you dread at the end of practice," he said. "They show up in big moments for us."

And while the Bears' offense is well-prepared to execute those extended drives, Johnson's priority remains making the most of each possession rather than focusing on how long a drive lasts.

"It's not something we stress," Johnson said. "We really talk more about getting explosive plays. I would rather have a one-play drive for a touchdown than a 10-play drive for a touchdown. But the ability to stay efficient, stay on schedule, I think that's something that good offenses show that they can do."

Johnson also pointed out that long drives leave little room for mistakes, since one misstep can bring an otherwise promising possession to an end.

"I prefer some more explosive plays be mixed in there, just so it takes the onus off just a little bit," Johnson explained. "It feels like you've got to be perfect throughout those long drives, and one mistake could derail you."

One factor contributing to the Bears' success in controlling possession has been their performance on third down on both sides of the ball.

The Bears convert 44.3% of their third downs, the fifth-best mark in the NFL, while their defense ranks No. 1 in the league, allowing conversions on just 23.8% of third downs.

What's made Chicago particularly dangerous on third down is that the offense hasn't just been converting in those situations. Some of the Bears' most explosive plays this season have come when they've needed to move the chains.Williams connects with Odunze on a spectacular third-down throw vs. Patriots

Kalif Raymond breaks loose for a big gain to set up Bears touchdown vs. Seahawks

Luther Burden III comes up big with a 24-yard touchdown on 3rd and 17

Time of possession might not be a statistic Ben Johnson puts much emphasis on, but the Bears' ability to win on third down and sustain drives allows Chicago to control the clock and dictate the tempo of games is certainly part of what makes this team special.

#2693. Player Grades: Lakers vs. Kings

For as good as Luka Dončić has been to start this season, it hasn't really felt like he's been carrying the Lakers along. He's been great on top of other players being good as well, leading more often than not to wins.

Damn. I was just called for a foul. Fitting for that game.

Anyway, Wednesday was the exception. The Lakers were mostly lethargic and flat to open the game, Luka included. Then, he exploded in the second quarter and really pulled the Lakers along. Austin Reaves was great alongside him as well, but the teams had little else.

At least they were solid enough defensively to make up for that. And they were playing Kings.

So, let's dive into the win. As always, grades are based on expectations for each player. A "B" grade represents the average performance for that player.

23 minutes, 3 points, 1 rebound, 2 assists, 2 steals, 2 blocks, 1-5 FG, 1-5 3PT, -4

A quiet game offensively from Thybulle. He was overdue for one, so nothing to really worry about.

20 minutes, 9 points, 2 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 steal, 1 block, 4 fouls, 1 turnover, 3-3 FG, 2-2 3PT, 1-2 FT, +3

When he wasn't fouling, LaRavia was one of the best Lakers on the floor. Unfortunately, he was fouling a lot in the first half, which forced him to the bench. But he was foul free in the second half and his impact was felt.

28 minutes, 15 points, 8 rebounds, 3 steals, 4 blocks, 6 fouls, 6-8 FG, 3-4 FT, -1

By his own admission, Kessler wasn't very good for the first three quarters. Domantas Sabonis had 10 points and 14 rebounds to Kessler's five points and three rebounds.

In the fourth quarter, that completely flipped. Kessler had 10 points, five rebounds and four blocks and Sabonis only had six points and three rebounds.

37 minutes, 21 points, 3 rebounds, 4 assists, 7-15 FG, 1-4 3PT, 6-6 FT, +14

This is a quiet great game from Reaves, who is willing and able to take that Robin role to Luka's Batman. The most impressive part of his game though isn't listed in that box score…because he had zero turnovers!

35 minutes, 40 points, 9 rebounds, 7 assists, 1 steal, 1 block, 1 foul, 6 turnovers, 11-23 FG, 6-13 3PT, 12-13 FT, +11

The level of shotmaking from Luka right now is up there with the best the league has ever seen. Some of these shots are just unfathomable and, yet, when they go up, you always believe they'll go in.

24 minutes, 6 points, 1 rebound, 1 assist, 1 steal, 1 foul, 1 turnover, 2-5 FG, 0-3 3PT, 2-2 FT, +19

A bit of a step back for Grimes after an encouraging game against the Heat. Considering how much the Lakers paid him, it'd be nice if he found his groove soon.

18 minutes, 2 points, 6 rebounds, 1 assist, 1 block, 1 foul, 1 turnover, 0-4 FG, 0-3 3PT, 2-2 FT, +17

This was a pretty poor Mamu game. Missing back-to-back threes from the top of the key early in the game turned out to be an omen.

22 minutes, 10 points, 4 rebounds, 1 assist, 2 steals, 4 fouls, 3-7 FG, 1-4 3PT, 3-3 FT, +12

The good news for the Lakers is that when a wing has a poor game as Thybulle did or LaRavia is in foul trouble, they have another one to step in and play well, which Ziaire did.

16 minutes, 10 points, 3 rebounds, 1 assist, 2 blocks, 4 fouls, 2 turnovers, 2-7 FG, 1-3 3PT, 5-6 FT, +2

Another sort of deceptive game as it looks like Sexton was good but it didn't feel that way while watching. Perhaps I'm in the minority because that's a good stat line.

Everyone but Vando played only in garbage time. Again, Vando was in for only one shift in the first half and the team was outrebounded 8-5, gave up three offensive boards and Vando committed two fouls, albeit on a bizarre possession with like four fouls called.

As he always does, Redick is rounding this team into form defensively. It's still a work in progress and this was just the Kings, but you can see this team improving on that end of the floor and, minus the rebounding, this was a good showing.

#2692. Kyle Shanahan confirms Jauan Jennings decided not to pursue trade back to 49ers

Yes, the 49ers tried to arrange a reunion with receiver Jauan Jennings.

Over the weekend, Jay Glazer of Fox Sports reported that the 49ers and Vikings had a deal in place, but that Jennings eventually decided to stay in Minnesota. On Wednesday, 49ers coach Kyle Shanahan confirmed it.

“The report was accurate, just not totally exactly detailed up,” Shanahan told reporters. “I mean, you can’t talk to other players on other teams. You’re not allowed to. We love Jauan. We have such a good relationship with Jauan and know him really well and we didn’t want to do it without talking to him first. So, we had to agree on some things to at least make it close enough to be able to talk to him.

“Then when we got on a FaceTime with J.J., it went well. It was awesome. We talked about a lot of stuff, and he ended up saying that he was happy with where he was. It wasn’t just like a definite ‘no’ first, but he was like, ‘Man, Kyle, I’m pretty happy here and liking stuff.’ And when I heard the way he talked and where we’re at, I agreed with him. So, it was a good conversation and something that was possible, but that’s why it didn’t go down.”

Jennings still hasn’t done much for the Vikings. In six games, he has six catches for 54 yards.

The fact that the Vikings allowed Jennings to talk to the 49ers shows that the Vikings were willing to move on. The fact that Jennings stayed has kept receiver Odell Beckham Jr. parked on the Minnesota practice squad.

#2691. Bears’ Jeffrey Simmons trade forces the NFC North’s hand

The NFC North is stacked with all four teams currently having a winning record.

This division is likely going to be a battle until the final week of the season. Monday night, the three other teams in the division got to see how the Chicago Bears are going to be set up for the stretch run and it had to terrify them all.

In their first game since their blockbuster trade for star defensive tackle Jeffrey Simmons, the Bears manhandled the defending Super Bowl champion Seattle Seahawks on the road. Simmons was a monster in his Chicago debut, making the team feel pretty good about their decision to send two first-round picks and a fourth-round pick to the Tennessee Titans for their defense changer.

Now, the other three teams have to respond by making big moves of their own. They might not all match the Simmons' deal in terms of star power, but aggression is needed around the NFC North.

Here is a move each of the 6-2 Bears' rivals can make before Tuesday's trade deadline:

The Lions are 5-2. They could use some help on defense, though. Linebacker is an obvious concern with the injuries to Jack Campbell and Jimmy Rolder. So a pursuit of a tackle machine like Jordyn Brooks of the Miami Dolphins could make sense. But we're going to look at the secondary for the Lions. They have had a need since they released 2024 first-round pick Terrion Arnold in the summer because of legal reasons.

So, we're going to pair the Lions with Philadelphia Eagles' cornerback Riq Woolen. He may be the best cornerback on the market and he could make an impact with Detroit. He won a Super Bowl with the Seattle Seahawks last year, so he knows how to play in important games. Woolen is 6-foot-4 and is a matchup concern. Plus, he is a free agent after the season, so he could come at a reasonable price. It wouldn't be a Simmons-like get, but Woolen would make the Lions better and that's the whole point of adding at the trade deadline.

The 5-3 Packers have been rumored to be interested in Indianapolis Colts' running back Jonathan Taylor. That's understandable with what is going on with running back Josh Jacobs. But acquiring Taylor might be complicated for the Packers. So, we're going elsewhere for their deadline addition.

It may not be as sexy, but Green Bay needs an upgrade on the offensive line. So, we're looking at the Eagles again. Howie Roseman is always open for business. So, the Packers should push for guard Landon Dickerson. He is a solid sixth-year pro and he would be an upgrade for a solid offense that needs help up front. Dickerson, 28, will be a free agent in 2028. But a third-day draft pick could do the trick for Green Bay. Again, this isn't an earth-rattling acquisition, but the Packers would be better with Dickerson.

No, we're not sending Kirk Cousins to the Vikings. That would be too pricey for the value.

Taylor is the call here. He's what the 6-1 Vikings need.

Taylor could change the Minnesota offense and make life easier for quarterback Kyler Murray. An offense featuring wide receiver Justin Jefferson and a tailback like Taylor would be dangerous. Coupled with the Vikings' defense, this team would be a legitimate Super Bowl threat and they would put pressure on the surging Bears.

The price tag may be multiple premium draft picks, but it would be worth it for the Vikings to make this deal.
